πολλὴν αὐτοῦ ἀρετὴν καὶ σωφροσύνην καὶ καθαρότητα, ἐπίσκοπος προχειρίζεται τῆς μεγάλης Ῥώμης, καὶ διεπράξατο μὲν καὶ ἄλλα πολλὰ ἄξια τῆς ἐκείνου ἀρετῆς, ἐξαιρέτως δὲ τὸ περὶ τῆς ὀρθῆς πίστεως · κατὰ γὰρ τὸν καιρὸν ἐκεῖνον σύνοδος συνεκροτήθη ἐν Χαλκηδόνι ἑξακοσίων τριάκοντα πατέρων, κατὰ τῶν αἱρετικῶν, τῶν λεγόντων ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ μίαν φύσιν · καὶ ἐπεὶ ἐδογμάτισαν τὴν ἀλήθειαν, οἱ δὲ αἱρετικοὶ οὐκ ἐπείθοντο, πᾶσιν ἤρεσεν, ἵνα ὁ ἐὰν εἴπῃ Λέων ὁ ἁγιώτατος πάπας Ῥώμης, θελήσωσι. Παρακληθεὶς οὖν ὑπὸ τῶν Πατέρων, καὶ τὸν θεὸν δυσωπήσας, ἔγραψεν ἐπιστολὴν ἑρμηνεύουσαν τὴν ἀλήθειαν, ἣν ὀρθοδοξίας στήλην ὠνόμασαν, δύο φύσεις ἐπὶ Χριστοῦ κηρύττουσαν · ὁ δὲ μέγας Λέων καὶ τὸ μετὰ ταῦτα καλῶς βιώσας, ἐν εἰρήνῃ ἐτελειώθη.  ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΙΘ´
*Αθλησις τῶν ἁγίων μαρτύρων Μαξίμου καὶ Θεοδότου.
Μάξιμος καὶ Θεόδοτος, οἱ τοῦ Χριστοῦ μάρτυρες, περιπατοῦντες κατὰ πᾶσαν πόλιν καὶ χώραν, μετὰ παῤῥησίας ἐκήρυττον τὴν ἀλήθειαν, καὶ τὸν Χριστὸν ὡμολόγουν θεὸν εἶναι ἀληθινὸν, καὶ Κύριον τοῦ παντός, καὶ δημιουργὸν τοῦ κόσμου · τὰ δὲ εἴδωλα χείρων ἔργα, καὶ τέχνης πλάσματα, διὰ λίθων καὶ ξύλων κατεσκευασμένα, Διὰ τοῦτο ἐκρατήθησαν παρὰ τῶν εἰδωλολατρῶν, καὶ παρεδόθησαν τῷ ἄρχοντι τῆς χώρας, καὶ ὑπ᾿ ἐκείνου ἐβασανίσθησαν ἰσχυρῶς, ἀρνήσασθαι τὸν Χριστὸν καταναγκαζόμενοι, καὶ προσκυνῆσαι τοῖς εἰδώλοις. Ὡς δὲ οὐκ ἐπείθοντο, ἐσύροντο δέσμιοι ἀπὸ πόλεως εἰς πόλιν, καὶ ἐθηριομάχουν. Εἶτα ἐξέσθησαν μετὰ σιδηρῶν ὀνύχων, καὶ τέλος καμίνου μεγάλης ἀφείσης, ἐνεβλήθησαν χαίροντες εἰς αὐτήν · καὶ εὐχαριστοῦντες, παρέδωκαν τὰς ἁγίας καὶ μακαρίας αὐτῶν ψυχὰς τῷ Κυρίῳ.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. Κ´
*Αθλησις τοῦ ἁγίου μάρτυρος Σαδώχ ἐπισκόπου καὶ τῶν σὺν αὐτῷ ἑκατὸν εἴκοσιτὼ ἁγίων.
Ὁ ἅγιος Σαδώχ ἦν ἐπίσκοπος ἐν Περσίδι · διδάσκων δὲ τὸν λαὸν τὴν εἰς Χριστὸν πίστιν, καὶ πολλοὺς πείθων προσέρχεσθαι τῷ ζῶντι καὶ ἀληθινῷ θεῷ, εἰς θυμὸν καὶ μεγάλην πικρίαν ἐκίνησε τοὺς πυρολάτρας Μάγους. Καὶ βουλομένων ἐκείνων ἀποστεῖλαι, καὶ κρατῆσαι τὸν ἅγιον, ὁρᾷ καθ᾿ ὕπνους τὸν πρὸ αὐτοῦ ἐπίσκοπον τὸν ἱερομάρτυρα Συμεών, ἱστάμενον εἰς μακρὰν σκάλαν, καὶ καλοῦντα αὐτὸν ἀνελθεῖν πρὸς αὐτόν, λέγοντα, ὅτι Καλεῖ σε ὁ Βασιλεὺς τῶν βασιλευόντων · τοῦτο δὲ ἐδήλου τὴν διὰ τοῦ μαρτυρίου ἀνάβασιν. Ἕως δὲ ταῦτα διηγεῖτο ὁ ἅγιος, ἀποστείλαντες οἱ Μάγοι ἐκράτησαν αὐτὸν καὶ τοὺς σὺν αὐτῷ ὄντας Χριστιανούς, καὶ ἔβαλον εἰς φυλακήν · καὶ ἀναγγείλαντες περὶ αὐτῶν Σαβωρὶφ τῷ βασιλεῖ Περσῶν, ἐξέβαλον αὐτοὺς τῆς φυλακῆς, καὶ ἐτιμωρήσαντο ἰσχυρῶς, καὶ τελευταῖον ἀπεκεφάλισαν ἅπαντας.


[322] {#pg.117.322}
tutes suas et continentiam atque puritatem episcopus magnæ Romæ ordinatus, multa quidem alia virtutibus suis digna gessit; sed præcipue in iis, quæ ad rectam fidem pertinent, refulsit. Coacta enim Chalcedonensi synodo sexcentorum triginta Patrum adversus eorum hæresim, qui unam in Christo naturam dicebant, cum ii veritatem prædicarent, nec tamen hæretici illis acquiescere vellent, placuit omnibus, ut quidquid Leo sanctissimus Romæ papa dixisset, id ratum firmumque haberetur. Rogatus itaque a Patribus, Deumque precatus, conscripsit epistolam, in qua veritatem exponens, duas in Christo naturas prædicabat, quam orthodoxiæ columnam appellant. Post hæc magnus Leo transacta sanctissime vita, in pace quie- vit.
EODEM MENSE. Die decima nona 202 Certamen sanctorum martyrum, Maximi et Theodoti.
Maximus et Theodotus Christi martyres cum civitates omnes regionesque obirent, libere veritatem prædicantes, Christum Deum verum esse, omniumque Dominum, et mundi factorem profitebantur; idola autem opera manuum, artisque figmenta ex lapidibus et lignis fabrefacta. Quare ab idolatris comprehensi, præfecto regionis traduntur: a quo gravissime excruciati, cum adigerentur Christum abnegare et idola colere, nec id facere vellent. vincti ab urbe in aliam pertrahuntur, et feris exponuntur: mox unguibus ferreis depexi ac demum in ardentem ignem fornacem injecti, læti et gratias agentes, sanctas ac beatas animas suas Domino tradiderunt.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima 203 Certamen sancti martyris Sadoch episcopi, et sociorum centum viginti octo sociorum.
Sanctus Sadoch in Perside episcopus, quod populum Christi fidem doceret, multos ad vivum verumque Deum adduceret, ad iram et indignationem, Magos ignis cultores commovit: quibus volentibus, satellites ad capiendum sanctum mittere, vidit idem sanctus in somnis sanctum martyrem Simeonem, suum in episcopatu decessorem, insidentem longæ scalæ, ipsumque, ut ad se veniret, compellantem, atque dicentem: Rex regum vocat te; quod significabat ipsius ascensum per martyrium. Cumque ista narraret Sanctus, qui missi fuerant Magi, comprehenderunt ipsum una cum sociis Christianis, et in vincula conjiciunt, causam eorum ad Saporem Persarum regem referentes: qui illos ex carcere eductos, ac gravissime tortos, postremo decollavit.


[323] {#pg.117.323}
Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Μνήμη τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Σωφρονίου ἐπισκόπου. Λείπει. Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Μνήμη τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Λέοντος ἐπισκόπου Κατάνης τῆς ἐν Σικελίᾳ.
Ὁ ἅγιος Πατὴρ ἡμῶν Λέων ὁ θαυματουργὸς ἐγένετο ἀπὸ τῆς πόλεως Ῥαβέννης, εὐγενῶν καὶ εὐσεβῶν γονέων υἱός· καὶ μαθὼν τὴν ἱερὰν πᾶσαν Γραφὴν, καὶ γενόμενος ἐνάρετος, ὡς καὶ θαύματα ποιεῖν, χειροτονεῖται πρεσβύτερος τῆς ἐν Ῥαβέννῃ ἐκκλησίας. Εἶτα γίνεται καὶ ἐπίσκοπος τῆς μητροπόλεως Κατάνης. Τοσοῦτον δὲ ἠγωνίσατο κατὰ τῶν αἱρετικῶν, ὡς νικῆσαι πάντας, καὶ καταισχῦναι, οὐ μόνον λόγοις, ἀλλὰ καὶ Γραφικαῖς δείξεσιν. Ἦν δὲ καὶ θαυματουργός· καὶ γὰρ τὸν μάγον Ἡλιόδωρον πολλοὺς ταῖς μαγείαις πλανῶντα, διὰ προσευχῆς πυρὶ κατέκαυσεν, καυχησάμενον πάντα δύνασθαι, ὡς μηδὲ πῦρ φοβεῖσθαι. Περὶ τούτου τοῦ ἁγίου καὶ οἱ βασιλεῖς Ῥωμαίων ἀκούσαντες, ἐθαύμασαν. Καὶ προσκαλεσάμενοι αὐτόν, ἠξίουν εὔξασθαι ὑπὲρ αὐτῶν· καὶ τυχόντες τῶν ἐκείνου εὐχῶν, μετὰ τιμῆς ἀπέλυσαν· ἀπελθόντα δὲ εἰς τὴν ἰδίαν ἐκκλησίαν προσελάβετο ὁ Κύριος.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΑ´ Μνήμη τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Ἀγάθωνος ἐπισκόπου Ῥώμης.
Καὶ οὗτος ὁ ὅσιος Πατὴρ ἡμῶν καὶ θαυματουργὸς Ἀγάθων ἐκ τῆς Ἰταλίας ἐγένετο, υἱὸς Χριστιανῶν γονέων, εὐλαβῶν καὶ εὐσεβῶν. Οἱ δὲ γονεῖς αὐτοῦ φιλοπονήσαντες ἐδίδαξαν αὐτὸν πᾶσαν Γραφὴν θεόπνευστον καὶ ὠφέλιμον. Τοσοῦτον γὰρ ὠφελήθη παρὰ τῆς θείας Γραφῆς, καὶ κατενύγη, ὡς μετὰ τὸ ἀποθανεῖν τοὺς γονεῖς αὐτοῦ, συναγαγεῖν πάντα τὸν πλοῦτον αὐτοῦ, καὶ προσκαλέσασθαι πτωχούς, καὶ ἐν μιᾷ ἡμέρᾳ διασκορπίσαι αὐτόν. Αὐτὸς δὲ ἀπελθὼν, καὶ γενόμενος ἐν ἡσυχίᾳ εἰς μοναστήριον, καὶ τὸ μοναχικὸν σχῆμα φορέσας, ἐδούλευε τῇ θεῷ, νυκτὸς καὶ ἡμέρας ὑπὲρ τοῦ κόσμου προσευχόμενος. Τοσοῦτον δὲ ἠγωνίσατο εἰς ἀρετήν, ὡς καὶ τὸ χάρισμα λαβεῖν τοῦ θαυματουργεῖν. Ἐπεὶ δὲ ἡ ἀρετὴ οὐ λανθάνει, γέγονε καὶ πάπας Ῥώμης· καὶ καλῶς διαπρέψας ἐν τῷ ἐπισκοπικῷ ἀξιώματι, καὶ πολλοῖς ἀγαθὸν ὑπόδειγμα τὸν ἑαυτοῦ βίον λιπὼν, ἐκοιμήθη.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΒ´ Μνήμη τοῦ ἁγίου Ἀθανασίου τοῦ Ὁμολογητοῦ. Λείπει.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΓ´ Ἄθλησις τοῦ ἁγίου ἱερομάρτυρος Πολυκάρπου ἐπισκόπου Σμύρνης.
Πολύκαρπος ὁ ἁγιώτατος μαθητὴς ἦν τοῦ ἁγίου ἀποστόλου καὶ εὐαγγελιστοῦ Ἰωάννου τοῦ θεολόγου· χειροτονεῖται δὲ ἐπίσκοπος εἰς Σμύρναν μετὰ τὸ τε-
Eodem die 204 Commemoratio sancti Patris nostri Sophronii episcopi. Deest. Eodem die. 205 Commemoratio sancti Patris nostri Leonis episcopi Catanæ in Sicilia.
Sanctus Pater noster Leo thaumaturgus, ex urbe Ravenna fuit, nobilium ac divitum parentum filius: sacris autem litteris oppido instructus, ac singulari virtute præditus, ut et miracula patraret, ecclesiæ Ravennatis presbyter ordinatur: deinde Catanæ metropoleos episcopus. Sic vero adversus hæreticos pugnavit, ut omnes convicerit et confuderit, non ratiociniis tantum, sed demonstrationibus etiam ex Scripturis petitis. Thaumaturgus autem fuit: nam Heliodoram magicis artibus multos decipientem, seque omnia posse gloriantem, ut vel ipsum ignem contemneret, precibus igne combussit. Hujus sancti fama permoti Romanorum imperatores, accitum rogarunt, ut pro se preces Deo offerret; cumque id ab eo impetrassent, honorifice ipsum dimisere. Cum vero ille ad suam ecclesiam rediret, migravit ad Dominum.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima prima 206 Commemoratio sancti Patris nostri Agathonis episcopi Romæ.
Iste sanctus Pater noster ac thaumaturgus Agatho ex Italia fuit, christianis piisque parentibus natus atque ab iis summo studio omnibus a Deo inspiratis Scripturis eruditus, ex quibus tantum utilitatis, compunctionisque cordis percepit, et mortuis parentibus congestas facultates suas omnes die uno convocatis pauperibus diviserit, ipse vero in monasterium secedens, et monastico habitu indutus, totum se divino cultui mancipaverit, die nocteque pro mundi salute orans. Sic autem in virtutibus profecit, ut et donum miraculorum acceperit. Quoniam vero virtus latere non potest, papa etiam Romæ factus est: cumque in episcopali dignitate optime se gessisset, multisque sui ipsius vitam tanquam exemplum imitandum reliquisset, obdormivit.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima secunda 207 Commemoratio sancti Atanasii Confessoris. Deest.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima tertia 208 Certamen sacrosancti martyris Polycarpi episcopi Smyrnæ.
Polycarpus sanctissimus Joannis Theologi, apostoli, et evangelistæ discipulus, Smyrnæ episcopus in demortui sacratissimi Bucoli episcopi locum


[325] {#pg.117.325}
λευτῆσαι Βουκόλον τὸν ἱερώτατον ἐπίσκοπον· καὶ γὰρ ἔτι ζῶν ὁ Βουκόλος προεφέτευσε περὶ αὐτοῦ, ὅτι Αὐτὸς μέλλει γενέσθαι δεύτερος ἐπίσκοπος Σμύρνης. Ὑπάρχοντος δὲ διωγμοῦ κατὰ τῶν Χριστιανῶν παρὰ Δεκίου τοῦ βασιλέως, ἐκρατήθη, καὶ προσήχθη τῷ ἀνθυπάτῳ, καὶ μετὰ πολλὰς βασάνους ἐτελειώθη· ἐποίησε δὲ θαύματα πολλά· καὶ γὰρ πρὸ τοῦ χειροτονηθῆναι, τὰς ἀναβεψάσας αὐτὸν γυναικὸς τὰς ἀποθήκας προσευξάμενος ἐγέμισε παντὸς ἀγαθοῦ, τὰς πρότερον κενωθείσας εἰς τὴν τῶν δεομένων χρείαν· καὶ μετὰ τὸ χειροτονηθῆναι, μέγαν ἐμπρησμὸν διὰ προσευχῆς ἔστησε· καὶ ἀβροχίας γενομένης, ὑετὸν κατήγαγε, καὶ ἄλλα πολλὰ παράδοξα Θεοῦ χάριτι ποιήσας, ἐκοιμήθη θαυματουργῶν ἕως τοῦ νῦν.
ΜΙΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΑ´ Ἡ ἀνάμνησις τῆς εὑρέσεως τῆς τιμίας κεφαλῆς τοῦ Προδρόμου.
Ὅτε ἀπεκεφαλίσθη ὑπὸ Ἡρώδου ὁ ἅγιος Ἰωάννης ὁ Πρόδρομος, ἐναπετέθη ἡ κεφαλὴ αὐτοῦ εἰς ἀγγεῖον ὀστράκινον, καὶ ἐκρύβη ἐν τῇ οἰκίᾳ τοῦ Ἡρώδου· μετὰ δὲ χρόνους πολλοὺς, δυςὶ μοναχοὶς ἀπελθοῦσιν εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα, τοῦ προσκυνῆσαι τὸν Κυρίου τάφον, ἐφάνη κατ᾿ ὄναρ ὁ ἅγιος Ἰωάννης μηνύων αὐτοῖς, ποῦ κεῖται ἡ τιμία κεφαλὴ αὐτοῦ. Καὶ εὑρόντες αὐτήν, εἶχον ἐν τιμῇ· ἐξ αὐτῶν δὲ ἀνελάβετο αὐτὴν κεραμεύς τις, καὶ ἤγαγεν εἰς τὴν Ἐμεσηνῶν πόλιν. Ὅτε δὲ ἐτελεύτησε, κατέλιπεν αὐτὴν τῇ δίᾳ ἀδελφῇ· καὶ ἔκτοτε κατὰ διαδοχὴν περιῆλθεν εἰς πολλούς. Ἐπὶ δὲ τῆς βασιλείας Μιχαὴλ, τοῦ ποιήσαντος τὴν ὀρθοδοξίαν, καὶ Ἰγνατίου πατριάρχου, ἀπεκομίσθη Θεοῦ χάριτι εἰς Κωνσταντινούπολιν· καὶ ἐναπετέθη μετὰ μεγάλης τιμῆς ἐν αὐτῇ, ὡς καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ πάντα ἅγια, καὶ τὰ τῶν ἁγίων λείψανα, εἰς σκέπην καὶ ἀσφάλειαν αὐτῆς.
Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Ἄθλησις τοῦ ἁγίου Ῥηγίνου ἐπισκόπου Σκοπέλων.
Ὁ ἅγιος Ἱερομάρτυς Ῥηγῖνος, ἐγένετο ἀπὸ τῆς χώρας Ἑλλάδος, γονέων Χριστιανῶν υἱός. Καὶ μαθὼν ὑπ᾿ αὐτῶν τὴν εὐσέβειαν, καὶ τὰ ἱερὰ Γράμματα, ἐγένετο ἐνάρετος καὶ φοβούμενος τὸν Θεὸν καὶ διὰ τὴν ἀρετὴν αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὴν πολλὴν εὐσέβειαν παρὰ τοῦ κατὰ τὸν τόπον ἐπισκόπου γίνεται διάκονος τῆς ἐν τῇ Ἑλλάδι Ἐκκλησίας· εἶτα πρεσβύτερος· εἶτα καὶ ἐπίσκοπος Σκοπέλων. Τοσοῦτον δὲ γέγονεν εἰς ἀρετὴν περίδοξος, ὡς καὶ εἰς τὴν ἐν Σαρδικῇ σύνοδον προσκληθῆναι, καὶ πάσας τὰς αἱρέσεις διὰ τοῦ λόγου αὐτοῦ, καὶ τῆς παρῥησίας ἀφανίσαι· εἶτα ὑποστρέψαι εἰς τὴν ἰδίαν ἐπισκοπήν. Καὶ μετὰ τοῦτο διωγμοῦ κινηθέντος κατὰ τῶν Χριστιανῶν, ἐκρατήθη παρὰ τοῦ τῆς Ἑλλάδος, ἄρχοντος καὶ μετὰ τὸ πολλὰ βασανισθῆναι, καὶ τιμωρηθῆναι, διὰ ξίφους τὴν ἱερὰν αὐτοῦ κεφαλὴν ἀπετμήθη.
ΜΙΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΕ´ Ἄθλησις τοῦ ἁγίου μάρτυρος Ἀλεξάνδρου μαρτυρήσαντος ἐν τῇ Θράκῃ Οὗτος ἦν ἐκ Ποτιόλων, μιᾶς τῶν εἰς Ῥώμην πό-


[326] {#pg.117.326}
ordinatus est: etenim Bucolus dum adhuc viveret, de eo prædixerat fore ut esset alter Smyrnæ episcopus. Commota autem a Decio imperatore adversus Christianos persecutione, comprehensus, et ad proconsulem adductus, post multos cruciatus occubuit. Plurima vero miracula edidit: etenim priusquam ordinaretur, exhaustum nutricis suæ penu bonorum omnium ad egentium utilitatem, fusis ad Deum precibus replevit. Postquam autem ordinatus est, magnum incendium oratione exstinxit: et siccitatis tempore imbrem impetravit. Cumque alia plurima portenta, Dei gratia, fecisset, obdormivit, miraculorum dono ad hunc diem illustratus.
EODEM MENSE Die vicesima quarta Commemoratio inventionis pretiosi capitis Præcursoris.
Cum sanctus Joannes Præcursor ab Herode decollatus est, inclusum ejus caput in vase fictili, in ejusdem Herodis domo latuit. Post multos autem annos monachis duobus Hierosolyma ad sepulcrum Domini adorandum profectis apparuit in somnis sanctus Joannes, indicans eis, ubi pretiosum ipsius caput jaceret: quod cum reperissent, in magno honore habuere. Ex iis id receptum figulus quidam in Emesenorum civitatem detulit Ab hoc postea, cum moreretur, sorori ipsius relictum est; et deinceps per manus plurimorum traditum, in ea urbe permansit, quod temporibus Michaelis imperatoris qui orthodoxiam restituit, et Ignatii patriarchæ, Constantinopolim, Dei gratia, translatum fuit, ibique honorificentissime depositum, sicut et cætera omnia sanctorum lipsana, in ejusdem urbis præsidium et firmitatem.
Eodem die Certamen sancti Rhegini episcopi Scopulorum.
Sacrosanctus martyr Rheginus ex regione Græciæ fuit, Christianorum parentum filius, a quibus pietatem et sacras litteras edoctus, virtutibus, ac timore Dei præditus evasit. Quare ob insignem ipsius pietatem ab ejus loci episcopo diaconus in ecclesia Græciæ ordinatus est; deinde presbyter, demum Scopulorum episcopus. Eam autem virtutum famam sibi comparavit, ut ad Sardicensem synodum vocatus, in ea hæreses omnes dicendo retuderit. Postea ad suum episcopatum regressus, excitata adversus Christianos persecutione, a Græciæ præfecto comprehensus, post multos cruciatus, capite plexus est.
EODEM MENSE Die vicesima quinta Certamen sancti martyris Alexandri, qui in Thracia martyrium subiit.
Iste ex Puteolis fuit, quæ urbs est apud Romam,


[327] {#pg.117.327}
λεως, ἐπὶ Μαξιμιανοῦ τοῦ βασιλέως, καὶ Τιβεριανοῦ ἡγεμόνος. Κρατηθεὶς δὲ παρὰ τοῦ ἡγεμόνος, ἠναγκάζετο προσκυνῆσαι τοῖς εἰδώλοις. Ὁ δὲ ἅγιος, ἀντὶ τοῦ προσκυνῆσαι τοῖς εἰδώλοις, μᾶλλον ὕβρισε καὶ αὐτὰ, καὶ τὸν ἡγεμόνα, εἰπὼν πρὸς αὐτὸν, ὅτι καὶ αὐτὰ μάταιά εἰσι, καὶ σὺ ὁ πιστεύων εἰς αὐτὰ ἄθλιος καὶ ταλαίπωρος. Διὰ τοῦτο ἐδέθη ἐξ ἄκρων χειρῶν, καὶ ἐκρεμάσθη. Καὶ εἰς τοὺς πόδας αὐτοῦ προσεδέθη λίθος βαρύς· καὶ μετὰ τοῦτο λυθεὶς, καὶ ἀπαχθεὶς ἐν Καρθαγέννῃ, πάλιν κρεμασθεὶς ἐξέσθη. Καὶ πάλιν ἀχθεὶς εἰς Μαρκιανούπολιν, μετὰ λαμπάδων πυρὸς τὸ πρόσωπον καίεται· καὶ ἄλλας πολλὰς βασάνους καὶ τιμωρίας ἀνδρείως, ὡς Χριστοῦ μάρτυς, ὑπομείνας, ἐν τῇ Θράκῃ ἀπελθὼν, ἀπεκεφαλίσθη, ἀπολαβὼν στέφανον ἄφθαρτον, καὶ ζωὴν αἰώνιον παρὰ Χριστοῦ τοῦ θεοῦ ἡμῶν.
Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Μνήμη τοῦ ἐν ἁγίοις Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Ταρασίου ἀρχιεπισκόπου Κωνσταντινουπόλεως.
Ταράσιος ὁ ἐν ἁγίοις Πατὴρ ἡμῶν ἐγένετο τῆς Κωνσταντινουπόλεως καὶ γέννημα καὶ θρέμμα, τὴν ἀξίαν ἀσηκρῆτις· ἀλλὰ διὰ τὴν γνῶσιν καὶ τὴν ἀρετὴν αὐτοῦ χειροτονεῖται πατριάρχης Κωνσταντινουπόλεως. Ὅστις καὶ τὴν τῶν σεπτῶν εἰκόνων προσκύνησιν ἐδογμάτισε· καὶ δι᾿ αὐτοῦ ἡ βασίλειος ἀρχὴ, καὶ ἡ Ῥωμαϊκὴ ἐξουσία πρὸς τὰς παραδόσεις τῶν ἁγίων ἀποστόλων, καὶ τῶν ἑπτὰ οἰκουμενικῶν συνόδων ἐπανῆλθε, καὶ πᾶσαι αἱ ἐκκλησίαι τοῖς ὀρθοδόξοις συνηνώθησαν. Ζήσας δὲ οὕτως εὐσεβῶς, καὶ τοῖς βασιλεῦσι τίμιος γενόμενος, καὶ μοναστήριον ἐν τῷ πέραν τοῦ στενοῦ οἰκοδομήσας, καὶ πλῆθος μοναστῶν συστησάμενος, καὶ τοὺς πτωχοὺς ἐλεήσας, καὶ καλῶς κυβερνήσας τὴν ἐκκλησίαν ἐπὶ ἔτη εἴκοσι δύο, καὶ μῆνας δύο, ἐν εἰρήνῃ ἐτελειώθη, καὶ κατετέθη ἐν τῷ αὐτῷ μοναστηρίῳ.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚϚ´ Μνήμη τοῦ ἁγίου Πορφυρίου ἐπισκόπου Γάζης.
Ὁ ἅγιος Πορφύριος ἐγένετο ἀπὸ τῆς πόλεως Θεσσαλονίκης, υἱὸς γονέων πλουσίων· καταλιπὼν δὲ καγονεῖς, καὶ πλοῦτον, ἐπὶ βασιλείας Ἀρκαδίου, καὶ Ὁνωρίου, ἀπῆλθεν εἰς Αἴγυπτον, καὶ ἐγένετο μοναχός. Εἶτα ἐλθὼν εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα, ἔπειθεν Ἰουδαίους καὶ Ἕλληνας προσέρχεσθαι τῷ Χριστῷ, σμίλην μεταχειριζόμενος, καὶ δέρματα ῥάπτων, καὶ τρέφων καὶ ἑαυτὸν καὶ τοὺς πένητας. Εἶτα χειροτονεῖται πρεσβύτερος ὑπὸ Πραΰλου πατριάρχου Ἱεροσολύμων. Καὶ τῶν Γαζαίων ζητησάντων αὐτόν, χειροτονεῖται ἐπίσκοπος ὑπὸ Ἰωάννου ἀρχιεπισκόπου Καισαρείας τῆς Φιλίππου. Καὶ πλεῖστα θαύματα ποιήσας, πολλοὺς τῶν Ἑλλήνων καὶ τῶν Ἰουδαίων ἐβάπτισε· καὶ πάντας τοὺς ναοὺς τῶν εἰδώλων καταστρέψας, ἐκκλησίας ἁγίων ἔκτισε, καὶ οὕτω καλῶς βιώσας πρὸς θεὸν ἐξεδήμησα, κληρονομήσας τὴν αἰώνιον ζωήν.
temporibus Maximiani imperatoris, et Tiberiani præfecti. Comprehensus autem a præfecto, et idola adorare compulsus, tantum abfuit, ut ea coleret, ut potius contumeliis tam idola. quam præfectum oneraret, inquiens : Et vana illa sunt, et vanus es ipse, miser et infelix, qui in ea credis. Quamobrem vinctis pendens manibus, appensisque ad pedes saxis, torquetur : deinde solutus, et Carthaginem ductus, rursus in equuleum agitur et depectitur. Inde Marcianopolim tractus, facibus in facie aduritur. Deimum cum multos alios cruciatus fortiter ut Christi martyr, pertulisset, in Thracia decollatur, coronam incorruptibilem vitamque æternam a Christo Deo nostro recipiens.
Eodem die 202 Commemoratio sancti Patris nostri Tarasii archiepiscopi Constantinopolitani.
Sanctus Pater noster Tarasius Constantinopoli natus et educatus, dignitate a Secretis erat : sed ob rerum cognitionem, multamque virtutem patriarcha Constantinopolitanus ordinatus est : qui et sanctarum imaginum cultum propugnavit, et opera sua id effecit, ut Romanum imperium ad sanclorum apostolorum traditiones, et ad septem œcumenicarum synodorum decreta accederet, et omnes ecclesiæ cum orthodoxis conjungerentur. Hunc itaque in modum religiose vixit, ab imperatoribus in honore habitus ; ædificatoque ultra freti angustias monasterio, ac multis monachis in ipso constitutis, liberalitate in pauperes insignis, cum ecclesiam annis viginti duobus et mensibus duobus optime rexisset, in pace quievit, et in suo monasterio sepultus fuit.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima sexta 203 Commemoratio sancti Porphyrii episcopi Gazæ.
Sanctus Porphyrius Thessalonicæ divitibus parentibus natus, iis ac opibus relictis, in Ægyptum secedens, Archadio et Honorio imperatoribus, monachus factus est. Deinde Hierosolymam petens, Judæis Græcisque auctor erat, ut Christo se adjungerent, cum iterum scalprum gereret, et pelles consueret, eaque arte seipsum, et pauperes sustentaret. Tum a Praylo patriarcha Hierosolymitano presbyter ordinatur : demum Gazensium precibus a Joanne Cæsareæ Philippi archiepiscopo episcopo Gazæ consecratur : qui cum plurima miracula edidisset, multos Græcorum Judæorumque baptizavit, omnibusque idolorum fanis eversis, ecclesias sanctorum excitavit, atque hunc in modum transacta sanctissime vita, ad Deum migravit, vitam æternam hæreditate capiens.


[329] {#pg.117.329}
Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Μνήμη τοῦ ὁσίου Στεφάνου τοῦ συστησαμένου τὸ γηροκομεῖον τοῦ ᾿Αρματίου. Δείπει.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΖ΄ Μνήμη Προκοπίου, καὶ Βασιλείου, τῶν ὁμολογητῶν, τῶν συνάθλων, καὶ συμμαθητῶν.
Οὗτοι ὑπῆρχον ἐπὶ τῆς βασιλείας Λέοντος τοῦ Εἰκονομάχου. Καταλιπόντες δὲ τὸν κόσμον, καὶ τὰ ἐν κόσμῳ, ἐγένοντο μοναχοί· καὶ καλῶς ἀσκήσαντες πρότερον, ὕστερον τῆς αἱρέσεως τῆς κατὰ τῶν ἁγίων εἰκόνων κινηθείσης, σπουδαίως ἀντέστησαν τοῖς εἰκονομάχοις. Ὅθεν καὶ κρατηθέντες, καὶ πολλὰ τιμωρηθέντες, οὐκ ἐνέδωκαν· ἀλλὰ τὴν ἀλήθειαν ἐκήρυττον μέχρι θανάτου. Διὸ καὶ ἐξέσθη ὁ ἅγιος Βασίλειος τὸ σῶμα πᾶν καὶ τὸν τράχηλον, καὶ τῇ φυλακῇ παρεδόθη. ᾿Απεκλείσθη δὲ καὶ ὁ ἅγιος Προκόπιος· τοῦ δὲ τυράννου τελευτήσαντος, ἀπελύθησαν· καὶ ἐκ τῆς φυλακῆς ἐξελθόντες, τῆς ὁμοίας ἐπεμελοῦντο διαγωγῆς, καὶ πολλοὺς πρὸς ἀρετὴν ἐπαλείφοντες, καὶ πρὸς τὴν ὀρθόδοξον πίστιν ἐπανάγοντες, ἐν διαφόροις καιροῖς, ἕκαστος πρὸς τὸν θεὸν, ὃν ἐκ βρέφους ἐπόθησαν, ἐξεδήμησαν· ἀγαθὸν ὑπόδειγμα καταλιπόντες πολλοῖς τὸν ἐνάρετον βίον αὐτῶν.
ΜΗΝΙ ΤΩ ΑΥΤΩ. ΚΗ΄. ῎Αθλησις τοῦ ἁγίου μάρτυρος Νέστορος.
Ὁ ἅγιος μάρτυς Νέστωρ ὑπῆρχεν ἐπὶ τῆς βασιλείας Δεκίου τοῦ παρανόμου καὶ ἀδικωτάτου βασιλέως, καὶ Ποπλίου ἡγεμόνος ἐκ τῆς πόλεως Πέργης τῆς Παμφυλίας· Χριστιανῶν δὲ γονέων ὑπάρχων υἱόν, καὶ ὑπ᾿ αὐτῶν τὰ ἱερὰ γράμματα μαθών, ἐπεστόμιζε τοὺς ῞Ελληνας ἐκ τῶν θείων Γραφῶν, καὶ πολλοὺς ἐπὶ τὸν θεὸν ἐπιστρέφων ἐβαπτίζεν. Διαβληθεὶς δὲ ἐκρατήθη παρὰ τοῦ ἄρχοντος Εἰρηνάρχου, καὶ ἤχθη πρὸς τὸν ἡγεμόνα· καὶ ὁμολογήσας ἐνώπιον αὐτοῦ τὸν Χριστὸν θεὸν ἀληθινὸν καὶ δημιουργὸν τοῦ κόσμου, πρῶτον μὲν τύπτεται ἰσχυρῶς· ἔπειτα ξέεται· καὶ τελευταῖον, ὡς οὐκ ἐπείθετο ἀρνήσασθαι τὸν Χριστόν, προσηλοῦται σταυρῷ. Καὶ κρεμάμενος ἐδίδασκε τοὺς παρεστῶτας τὴν ὁδὸν τῆς ἀληθείας· καὶ ρούσης, ζωῆς ἐξέπνευσεν.
Τῇ αὐτῇ ἡμέρᾳ Μνήμη τοῦ ὁσίου Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Θαλελαίου, Οὗτος ἐγένετο μὲν ἀπὸ Κιλικίας· τὴν ἀσκητικὴν δὲ πολιτείαν ἀγαπήσας, τὴν πόλιν κατέλαβε Γαβάλων· καὶ εὑρὼν τόπον τινὰ πλησίον τῆς πόλεως, ὅστις καὶ οἰκητήριον ἦν δαιμόνων, ἐν αὐτῷ ἔκτισε μικρὰν οἰκίαν· καὶ τοὺς δαίμονας ἀποδιώξας, ἐν τῇ αὐτῇ οἰκίᾳ τὸν θεὸν ἱκέτευε, νηστείαις, καὶ ἀγρυπνίαις, καὶ ξηροκοιτίαις, καὶ κακουχίαις σαρκὸς ἑαυτὸν καταδαπανῶν. ᾿Ιδόντες δὲ οἱ δαίμονες τὴν ἀρετὴν αὐτοῦ, ἐπειράθησαν ἐκφοβῆσαι αὐτόν· οὐκ ἠδυνήθησαν δέ· διὰ γὰρ προσευχῆς ἐποίει αὐτοὺς ἀφαντους, καὶ τότε ἐμάνησαν· καὶ ἤρξαντο ἀνασπᾶν τὰ δένδρα, τὰς ἐλαίας, καὶ τὰς συκᾶς, ἐπεὶ δὲ οὐδὲ εἰς
PATROL. GR. CXVII.


[330] {#pg.117.330}
Eodem die 214 Commemoratio sancti Stephani, qui condidit valetudinarium Armatii. Deest.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima septima 215 Commemoratio Procopii et Basilii confessorum, et condiscipulorum, qui una martyrium subierunt.
Isti Leone Iconomacho imperatore claruerunt : qui, mundo mundanisque rebus valedicentes, monachi facti sunt : cumque prius asceticam vitam diligenter excoluissent, excitata postea adversus sacras imagines hæresi, Iconomachis fortiter sese opposuerunt. Quare comprehensi, multisque cruciatibus vexati, cum nihil de pristini animi sententia remitterent, sed usque ad extremum spiritum veritatem prædicarent; Basilius quidem toto corpore et collo excarnificatus, custodia traditur, in quam et sanctus Procopius detrusus est. Sed mortuo tyranno et vinculis educti, ad pristina studia rediere, multosque ad virtutem excitantes et ad orthodoxam fidem revocantes, tandem diversis temporibus ad Deum, quem ab ineunte ætate amaverant, migrarunt, vitam suam optimum cæteris virtutem exemplum relinquentes.
EODEM MENSE. Die vicesima octava 216 Certamen sancti Nestoris.
Sanctus martyr Nestor, Decio impio et iniquo imperatore, et Publio præfecto, Perge in Pamphylia Christianis parentibus natus, ab iis sacras litteras edoctus, Græcorum os sacræ Scripturæ testimoniis obstruens, complures ad Deum conversos baptizavit. Quare delatus, et ab Irenarcho prætore comprehensus, præfecto sistitur : cumque coram eo Christum verum Deum, mundique opificem confessus esset, primum flagris acerbissime cæditur, deinde excarnificatur; demum cum Christum ejurare nollet, in crucem agitur : ibique pendens, et astantes viam veritatis vitæque præsentis contemptum docens, obdormivit. οὕτω διδάσκων τοὺς παρεστῶτας καταφρονεῖν τῆς πα-
Eodem die 217 Commemoratio sancti Patris nostri Thalelæi. Iste ex Cilicia fuit : qui, asceticæ vitæ studio in urbem Gabalorum profectus in loco quodam urbi proximo, quod dæmonum habitaculum erat, cellulam ædificavit : fugatisque dæmonibus, Deum ibi colebat, jejuniis, vigiliis, humi cubationibus, et carnis maceratione seipsum afflictans. Videntes autem dæmones tantos illius in virtute progressus, conati sunt timorem ei injicere : sed cum operam perderent, nam oratione Thalelæi evanescere cogebantur, furore perciti, arbores, oleas, et ficus sursum trahere cœperunt. Cumque sanctus nihil hisce moveretur, nocturno tempore cum clamore
11
Because of this the Persians seized him, and tortured him severely. And because of the many bonds, he was like one dead, barely breathing. And lying there, he opened his eyes, and said to those who stood by: "Arise, and make prayers to God; for behold, I see a ladder reaching to heaven, and fiery men upon it, saying to me: 'Come with us, and we shall lead you into a city full of joy, and gladness, and light.'" With these words he breathed his last: and his holy soul flew up to the heavenly city, which he had seen. But his body lies buried in Persia, awaiting the final and terrible resurrection, when he too shall rise with all others, and enjoy the immortal kingdom of Christ our God.
On the same month. The second day.
Commemoration of the holy and blessed Philaret the Merciful.
In the reign of the most religious Empress Irene, this man flourished; originating from the region of Paphlagonia, he possessed indeed quite ample wealth, which he had gathered for himself through his own labor; but afterwards, through diabolical envy, he was reduced to poverty, yet he did not therefore forget almsgiving. From one example of his beneficence, it will be made plain how incredible was his liberality towards the poor. Once, the ox of his neighbor had wandered off: the neighbor approached Philaretus, and found him in his field sowing with oxen joined under the yoke. But Philaretus, seeing the man overcome with sorrow, unyoked one of the oxen and gave it to him, and he himself took the yoke upon himself with the other ox and returned home. God, intending to reward this virtue, brought it about that the empress arranged for Maria, his granddaughter, to be wedded to her son Constantine. By which it came to pass that his family was enriched. But he himself became more inclined towards the poor, and flourishing until old age, he died, and was buried in the monastery of Kriseos.
On the same day.
Commemoration of the holy prophet Habakkuk.
This man was of the tribe of Simeon from the region of Judea: he prophesied concerning the captivity and the destruction of Jerusalem, the city of the Hebrews, and about the slaughter and the servitude of the people. But when Nebuchadnezzar, the king of the Persians, had come against Jerusalem, he fled and went to a foreign land; and as soon as the Persians retreated, and those of the Jews who remained had gone down into Egypt, he returned himself also to his own land, and was serving the reapers of his field: and when he was once bringing lunch to them, he said to his own household: "I am going a long path: but if I delay for a longer time, take food yourselves to the reapers." Having said these things, he was caught up by an angel to Babylon, which was distant twelve hundred and sixty-five miles, and he gave the food to the prophet Daniel, who was shut up in the pit: and being carried home again, he brought another lunch to the reapers, having disclosed the matter to no one.
After having prophesied many other things, he died and was buried.
On the same day.
The contest of the holy martyr Abibus.
Under the reign of the emperor Licinius, this man was a deacon and a teacher of Christ’s Church, and as he instructed all who came to him in the word of truth. Having learned of this, the emperor, in his anger, sent men to arrest him and subject him to torture. Then, having kindled a great furnace, he threw him into it. In it, the saint, singing psalms and giving thanks, met his end; those [who were] Christians secretly carried him away and, having anointed him with myrrh and incense, buried him with psalms, hymns, and spiritual songs along with the holy confessors Gurias and Samonas, who were distinguished by the miracle that they saved from death the girl to whom a certain Goth, having sworn in the temple of these same saints that he would keep his marriage vows, had soon deceived and reduced to slavery, and what is more, had thrown alive into a tomb; but these saints, since they answered her (for it was in their temple that the Goth had sworn, and thus he had taken the girl), rescued her from death.
OF THE SAME MONTH.
THE THIRD DAY. [г]
The contest of the holy great-martyr Theodore, Bishop of Alexandria.
The people in Alexandria of Egypt, and especially the Greeks, being without faith, were bitter, harsh, and cruel toward the saints, and because of this they brought about [the deaths of] many martyrs. Among these martyrs was this holy Theodore, because the people of Alexandria, having learned that this holy Theodore confessed Christ and believed in Him, and taught the Greeks to hold Him as God, rushed upon him with bitterness; and having seized him, first they punished him severely. Afterward, having woven a crown of thorns, they placed it upon his head; and they struck his eyes, and he was mocked with jokes and contempt. Then, having been bound, he was thrown into the sea. But coming out of it unharmed, by the order of the prefect he was beheaded; and his holy and blessed spirit ascended to the heavens; but his precious relics were deposited in that same city of Alexandria, providing healings to those who approach them until the present day.
On the same day.
Commemoration of the holy and glorious prophet Sophonias.
This holy and glorious prophet Sophonias was from the tribe of Simeon, the son of the patriarch Jacob and brother of the most beautiful Joseph, and fulfilling all the commandments of God, and walking in his precepts, he received the gift of prophecy. And he prophesied concerning the destruction and desolation of Jerusalem, and concerning the extermination of the Jews; and that, once the Hebrews had been completely rejected, a special people of God would be chosen from the Gentiles, with the utmost disgrace and shame of the wicked, and the utmost glory and honor of the righteous; which finally Christ the Lord...
will render to everyone according to his works. After Sophonias had predicted all these things, he died, and his body was buried in his house, and there it lies, awaiting the final resurrection. He was very similar to John the Theologian in appearance and physical features, except that his chin was a little rounder.
On the same day.
Commemoration of Saint Theodulus.
Saint Theodulus was from the island of Cyprus. From his youth, having renounced the affairs of this world, he embraced Christ with all his soul and became a monk. He was so taken with enthusiasm for the poverty and humility of Christ that, instead of a soft mattress, he used stones, and, lying upon them, he would take his sleep. Thus, therefore, by much continence and by macerating his body with sleeping on the ground, he received many other spiritual gifts; but most especially this one: that he could peer into the thoughts of all men. Therefore, if anyone spoke something other than what he believed, he would rebuke him and declare whatever that person had conceived in his mind. Indeed, by feigning folly, he would also rebuke the deeds of each individual, converting sinners to repentance. Having lived in this way, and having saved many through his conduct and doctrine, he passed away in peace, and his remains, deposited in Cyprus, pour forth healings.
In the same month.
Day the fourth.
 The Contest of Saint Barbara, martyr.
Saint Barbara the martyr was from the East, the daughter of Dioscorus, a certain Gentile, during the times of the impious emperor Maximian. Her father, fearing that his daughter might be abducted on account of her extraordinary beauty, locked her up in a very high tower that he had built himself, and gave her idols to worship. But she, having smashed the idols and being inflamed with the love of Christ, worshipped Him. Whence also, when her father had ordered a bathhouse to be built and commanded the workmen to make one window in it, she, after her father’s departure, looking out from the tower, ordered three windows to be opened in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. Being discovered by her father, therefore, to be a Christian, she was handed over to the prefect to be tortured. Then, seizing an opportunity for escape, she rushed to the mountain, which her father himself, pursuing at full speed, overtook and beheaded with his own hands; but as he was descending from the mountain, fire fell from heaven and he was consumed.
In the same month.
Day the fifth.
Commemoration of our holy Father Sabas.
During the reign of Theodosius the Younger, this holy Sabas flourished, born in the region of Cappadocia to John and Sophia. From his earliest age, bidding farewell to his parents and the world, he went away and became a monk. But kindled by a desire for greater struggles and greater virtue,
he betook himself to the great Euthymius, by whom he was sent to the lower monastery to Saint Theoctistus, where he strove for virtue with such intensity that he not only surpassed all the monks but also gained fame among the Emperors Anastasius and Justinian; for he was sent to them as a mediator by the Patriarchs of Jerusalem. After he had instructed many in virtue, founded many monasteries, and performed many miracles—of which this one is remembered, that he brought forth water from the earth—he migrated to the Lord at the age of eighty-five.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The sixth day
 Commemoration of our holy father Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia.
This great pontiff and wonder-worker flourished under the Emperor Constantine the Great, serving as bishop in the city of Myra in Lycia. At first, he lived a monastic life, devoting himself with all his soul to virtue. Afterwards, having been ordained bishop, he performed many miracles. He liberated from death three men who had been falsely accused, cast into chains, and were being led to punishment; for he ran to them, and with the sword now hanging over their necks, he snatched it from the hands of the executioner and set them free. Similarly, three others had been falsely accused of conspiracy against the Emperor Constantine by the praefect Ablabius, and having been locked in the praetorium, they were about to be beheaded when they called upon Saint Nicholas. He came to Constantinople in a dream, and commanded the emperor and the praefect to release them. After performing many other miracles, he passed away in peace.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The seventh day
Commemoration of our holy father Ambrose, Bishop of Milan.
Ambrose, the admirable, was from the city of Rome, of the senatorial order, in the times of Constantius and Constans, sons of Constantine the Great. Because of his many virtues and his justice, he was made Bishop of Milan, and he lived until the times of Theodosius the Great. When he was inflamed with divine zeal and possessed a free spirit, and saw that Theodosius the Great, having been vehemently moved, had sent an army and ordered the slaughter of the people of Thessalonica who had sinned against the emperor, he stood at the entrance of the temple on the feast of the Nativity of Christ, and when the emperor wished to enter to pray and partake of the sacred mysteries, he prohibited his entry, saying, "The Church of God does not admit homicide, and especially those who have slaughtered Christians of the same race as himself." The emperor marveled at the saint, and having honored him, he withdrew, affirming that Ambrose was truly a bishop. He, indeed, after having done many other things worthy of memory, died and was buried.
ON THE SAME MONTH. The Eighth Day.
A 14 The contest of the holy martyr Athenodorus. This saint was from Mesopotamia of Syria, a pious monk and fearing God, in the times of the Emperor Diocletian. Being denounced by the idolaters because he held Christ for God, he was apprehended by Eleusius the governor, and bound with ropes to two columns, and burned with lighted torches. Then they placed burning iron spheres in his armpits, and stretched him out upon a huge fiery plate, equal in size to a door; soon they enclosed him within a bronze ox beneath which a flame was set. But he, delivered by angelic ministry from all these things, brought eighty men to the faith of Christ and made them Christians. The prefect therefore ordered that he be beheaded. And when the executioner threatened his neck with a drawn sword, the hand which held the sword was suddenly torn off from the shoulder and fell to the ground together with the blade. Wherefore no one else dared to approach him. But the saint made an end of his life.
ON THE SAME MONTH. The Ninth Day.
15 The conception of the holy Anne, mother of the Theotokos. When our Lord and God wished to prepare for Himself an animated temple and a holy house as His own dwelling, He sent His angel to the just Joachim and Anne, from whom He had destined His own Mother according to the flesh to proceed, and He announced the conception of the unfruitful and sterile one, so that He might produce faith in the fertility of the Virgin. She was conceived, therefore, and the holy Virgin Mary was born, not, as some say, at seven months, or without the work of a man, but with nine full months completed, by promise indeed, yet from the conjunction of a man: for our Lord and God Jesus Christ alone was born of the holy Virgin without a man, and without commixtion and seed, in a manner known to Him alone, but to us ineffable and inexplicable; and although He was perfect God, He perfectly assumed all things which pertain to the economy of the flesh, just as He created and fashioned human nature from the beginning.
On the same day.
16 Commemoration of our holy Father John, bishop, who afterwards withdrew into the Laura of Saint Sabas. John, the servant of Christ, was born at Nicopolis in Armenia. After his parents had died, in the reign of the Emperor Marcian, having distributed his wealth to the poor, he became a monk. But because of his virtue, he was ordained bishop of Colonia by the Archbishop of Sebaste; and having spent ten years in the episcopate, he resigned it, set out for Jerusalem, and withdrew into the Laura of Saint Sabas: where he progressed so much in virtues, that at one time, walking and weary from the labor of a journey, he was caught up by divine power to his cell, which was five miles from
the place where he was in the middle of the road. But even when the Persians came against that region and arrived at the laura, they were blinded and could not see his cell. He asked God to see how the soul departs from the body; and he was granted his wish. And when he had lived in total a hundred and four years, he was perfected.
On the same day. 
Commemoration of the holy apostles from the seventy: Sosthenes, Apollos, Cephas, Tychicus, Epaphroditus, Caesar, and Onesiphorus.
Of these holy apostles, Sosthenes, whom the holy apostle Paul mentions, became bishop of Colophon; Apollos, whom the same apostle also mentions in the first epistle to the Corinthians, became bishop of Caesarea; Tychicus, of whom the same apostle also makes mention, became the second bishop after Sosthenes of Colophon; Epaphroditus, whom the same apostle also mentions, became bishop of Hadria; and Caesar, whom the same apostle also mentions, became bishop of Corone. All these, having lived well and guided their assigned Churches and people holily and religiously, and having endured many trials and tortures for Christ at the hands of the idolaters, were perfected, delivering their souls to the Lord, for whose sake they were willingly slaughtered. C
On the same day.
Commemoration of our holy Father Patapius.
Our holy Father Patapius was from Thebes in Egypt. Having become a monk, he departed to the desert and remained quiet there for many years. Afterward, leaving the desert, he arrived in Constantinople, where he performed many miracles: he restored sight to the blind through prayer, and healed one suffering from dropsy by the anointing of holy oil; he also cured another who had a cancer, which, as the physicians say, is an incurable disease. And he cast out and drove away demons not only from human bodies but also from many places where they were harming people. And he performed many other miracles worthy of memory and record. Being such a man, and honored by emperors and patriarchs, and loved by all for his virtue, he received the end of his present life in peace, and delivered his blessed soul to the Lord. His precious relics were laid to rest in Constantinople.
On the same day.
Commemoration of our holy Father Sophronius, bishop of Constantia in Cyprus.
Our holy Father Sophronius was born on Cyprus, the great island, of Christian and pious parents. By the excellence of his intellect, he was learned in many sciences, and, reading the holy Scripture diligently, he meditated on the words of the Lord by night and by day. And he advanced so much in virtue and piety, that he became worthy of many gifts
and to perform many miracles. Thus, even after the death of Damianos, the most holy archbishop of Cyprus, he was ordained archbishop of the most holy Church of Cyprus by the suffrages of all the people and the bishops. Having assumed the government of the Church, he showed himself to be a leader of the poor, a helper of orphans, a defender of widows, a deliverer of those who were suffering, and a covering for the naked. Having spent the whole of his life in this manner without offense and without stain, and having fulfilled the commandments of God as far as he was able, he departed to the Lord in extreme old age.
ON THE SAME MONTH. The Tenth Day.
The contest of the holy martyrs Menas, Hermogenes, and Eugraphos.
The holy martyr Menas lived in the time of the Emperor Maximian. When a certain matter of dispute and questioning arose in the city of Alexandria, he was sent by the emperor to settle the quarrels, as one who excelled in wisdom and eloquence. Having traveled to Alexandria, he removed all controversy, made the Christians more prompt and encouraged, and healed those who were suffering from any illness. When the emperor learned of this, he sent the prefect Hermogenes, assigning him the task of using every kind of torture to lead the holy Menas away from the Christian faith. And he first gouged out the eyes of Menas, and then cut out his tongue. But when he saw him restored to health, Hermogenes himself believed; he was baptized by the holy Menas, and immediately ordained a bishop by the bishops who had gathered there. When this was reported to the emperor, he himself came and ordered them, together with his notary Eugraphos who had embraced the faith, to be most severely tortured and finally killed by the sword.
On the same day. The contest of the saint and multi-victorious Gemellos the Paphlagonian.
This saint was from the region of Paphlagonia. And having heard that Julian the Apostate was in Ancyra in Galatia, he went to him, and reproached him for being an apostate and an enemy of Christ. Because of this, Julian was indignant and began to torture Gemellos; first, he constrained him with an iron and burning belt, and, dripping with much blood, he took him with him to the city of Edessa; there, with burning awls, he pierced his shoulders; then, having fixed iron nails into his head, he stretched out the holy man on the ground and bound him on four sides, and flayed his skin from his feet up to his head. Finally, he put him on a cross, fastening his hands and feet with nails: and thus the holy man departed to God, giving thanks until his final breath.
ON THE SAME MONTH. The Eleventh Day.
The contest of the holy martyrs Aithalas and Apsai.
These saints were from the region of the Persians: and Aithalas
indeed, being a priest of idols, came to know the faith in Christ in this manner. Falling ill, he resolved to go to the bishop of the Christians so that he might be cured: and having done so, he was immediately restored to health. Then he returned to his own city, that he might teach his relatives to become Christians. When the governor learned of this, he seized him, mutilated his ears, and threw him into prison. The holy deacon Apsai was also seized, and having confessed Christ, after being most bitterly tortured, was sent to the chief of the Magi together with the holy Aithalas. The chief of the Magi led them to the king: and when, in the presence of the king, they were questioned and urged to deny Christ, and yet could not be induced to do so, they finished by being beheaded.
Eodem die.
Commemoration of our holy Father Daniel the Stylite, who was in Anaplus.
This man was born in Mesopotamia of Syria during the reign of Leo the Great; when he was five years old, he departed to a monastery, and became a monk. After this, he went to the great Symeon the Stylite, who also blessed Daniel. Then, by divine revelation, having gone to Anaplus, he shut himself up in an idol temple, suffering many afflictions from the demons. Finally, having ascended a column, he became a Stylite: his constancy and firmness of soul could never be broken by any cold, any heat, or any force of winds. He therefore performed many miracles, and predicted the fires and changes of the city, and was dear to all, and so admirable that the Emperor Leo used to approach him to request his prayers, from whom he learned many other things useful for the soul, and then this above all, that he should show himself merciful toward those who were at fault. Having lived in this manner, he died.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The Twelfth Day.
Commemoration of our holy Father Spyridon, Bishop of Trimithus in Cyprus.
Our holy Father Spyridon lived during the reign of the Emperor Constantine, a man simple in his ways, humble of heart. He tended sheep. His wife having died, from whom he had received a daughter, he was ordained bishop, and became a renowned performer of miracles: for during a great drought, he obtained rain by his prayers; during a supreme scarcity of grain, he brought about an abundance of food; he converted a serpent into gold, and again, after the poor man had pawned the gold to procure food for himself, he turned it back into the nature of a serpent and sent it away; he caused a most rapid river to stand still. He also attended the Nicene Council and confounded the heretics. A certain woman had entrusted a deposit to his daughter; then she died, and when the deposit was sought, the old man asked the deceased woman, and
she replied, saying, “It is located in this place,” and having done many other things, she departed in peace.
On the same day.
The contest of Saint Synetus the martyr.
The martyr Synetus was from ancient Rome. While still young in age, he was committed to the Roman bishop by his parents so that he might learn the holy scriptures, and he became a most holy reader of the Church in Rome. Being virtuous, and teaching many piety and the true faith, and reproving and mocking the gods of the Greeks, and calling the worshippers of idols miserable and deluded, he was reported to the emperor Aurelian. Being seized by him and interrogated, he freely confessed Christ, and though he was beaten severely and tortured in various ways, he could not be induced to deny Christ; after many tortures, his holy head was cut off, and he rendered up his blessed soul to the Lord.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The thirteenth day.
The contest of the holy martyrs Eustratius, Auxentius, Eugenius, Mardarius, and Orestes.
At the time when Maximian and Diocletian stirred up the persecution against the Christians, the holy Eustratius was seized by Lysius the governor, and having been tortured with many cruelties; and with his feet fitted with sandals having sharp nails, he was forced to walk while being beaten with rods. Auxentius was most cruelly tortured and beheaded; his head remained hidden for a long time, but was eventually found lying on a tree, with a raven standing over it and making a sound as an omen. But Saint Mardarius also endured countless tortures, having nothing else on his tongue but, “I am a Christian.” Therefore, having had his shins pierced and being suspended by his head, with his lower limbs scorched, he ended his life. Furthermore, Saint Eugenius, having had his tongue and both hands cut off, and after his legs had been broken with rods, expired. But Saint Orestes, having revealed the cross that he carried hidden, was seized, and having been placed upon a red-hot iron bed, migrated to the Lord. Saint Eustratius, for his part, after many tortures, was cast into a furnace and departed.
On the same day.
The contest of Saint Lucia the virgin.
The martyr Lucia was born in Syracuse, on the great island of Sicily. She had a mother who was suffering from an issue of blood. And having taken her, she went to the city of Catania so that she might beseech the holy martyr Agatha, and that she might heal her. The holy martyr Agatha then visited the mother of Lucia, and prophesied concerning her that she would in the future undergo martyrdom for Christ. Having returned, they gave their property to the poor; but since she was striving to preserve her virginity, the man who wanted to marry her saw her as a wife, and...
denounced her to the governor of the city. Being seized, she was compelled to deny Christ. And since she was not persuaded, the governor ordered her to be carried off to a brothel. But she remained, by the grace of God, pure and untouched in that place, for God was guarding her. Finally, they lit a great furnace and threw her into the fire. And as she was not harmed, she was beheaded by them.
ON THE SAME MONTH. THE FOURTEENTH DAY.
The Contest of the holy martyrs Thyrsus and Leucius.
Decius being emperor, when the governor Combricius stirred up a great persecution against the Christians in the regions of Nicomedia, spilling the blood of Christians every day like water, Leucius, a Christian man who feared God, being grieved, went to the governor, saying, "Insatiable dog, how long will you pour out the blood of men like water, compelling them to worship stones and wood, which are devoid of mind and sense?" The governor, having become enraged, had him seized, stretched upon the rack, and after his flesh was torn, ordered him to be beheaded. Thyrsus, however, meeting the same governor, rebuked him because, having exhausted the blood of many mortals, he had not yet sated his own soul. For this reason, he also had him seized, and with his hands and feet bound, his eyes pierced, and his legs broken, he ordered him to be sawn in half with a saw. But before he could be rent asunder and divided into two parts, he yielded up his soul.
On the same day. The Contest of the holy martyrs Philemon, Apollonius, and their companions.
These saints originated from Thebes, a city of Egypt, under the emperor Diocletian. They were thirty-six in number. When they had been seized, one of them, Apollonius by name, who was a cleric, gave money to their guard, the citharoedist Philemon, and begged him to take his garments, go in his place, and worship and sacrifice to the idols. But Philemon, having donned the garments of Apollonius, when he went to sacrifice, confessed Christ. The governor, ignorant of this matter, said, "Call the citharoedist Philemon, so that by playing the cithara and by the sweetness of his song, he might induce the Christians to sacrifice to the idols." Philemon, therefore, being recognized, was tortured by the governor along with the others; eventually, the governor himself, along with all his household, accepted the faith of Christ. When Diocletian learned of this, he ordered him to be tortured, along with a certain wealthy man named Arrianus; finally, he ordered them all to be bound, enclosed in sacks, and cast into the sea.
On the same day. The discovery of the relics of the holy Arrianus and his protectors.
After having tortured the saints with various types of punishment in the holy...
prison, Diocletian threw the holy Arrianus, bound in chains, into a very deep pit in the earth, and filled the pit with a mound of earth. And he set his throne above it and allowed the soldiers to mock him, saying: "Let us see if Christ comes to Arrianus and brings him out of this pit." But scarcely had he returned to the palace and reclined, when he saw the stones and chains which Arrianus had worn, hanging from his bed, and Arrianus himself lying upon it. Wherefore the emperor, terrified, thinking him to be a magician, cast both him and the soldiers who had believed with him into the sea; their relics, placed in five sacks, were carried by dolphins to the shore of Alexandria, where they were recognized by the servants of Arrianus and honorably deposited in the city of Alexandria.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The fifteenth day.
The combat of the holy hieromartyr Eleutherius.
The holy martyr Eleutherius was from the great city of Rome, orphaned of his father from childhood; he was taught the faith of Christ by his mother, Anthia, which she had received from the great apostle Paul. Bishop Anicetus received him, and having instructed him in Christian letters, made him a cleric. He was soon ordained deacon and presbyter, and then bishop of Illyricum. Having been seized during the reign of the emperor Hadrian, and most grievously tortured, he was cast into a burning furnace by order of the prefect. But when the prefect perceived that he had escaped from it unharmed, he himself believed, and entering into the furnace, came out of it unscathed. Then indeed the prefect was beheaded, along with all the others who had believed in Christ. But the holy Eleutherius was cut to pieces by the executioners with the sword, and yielded up his spirit.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The sixteenth day.
The combat of the holy martyr Marinus.
This noble senator lived in the times of the emperor Macrinus. While he was still in the flower of his age, he was arrested for the sake of the Christian faith and compelled to perform sacrifices to idols. Desecrating this crime, he was suspended on the rack and torn by swords. Soon he was burned by fire, cast into a burning pan, and plunged into a boiling cauldron. But by the grace of God moving unscathed from all these things, he was sentenced to the beasts, with lions and bears set upon him; and having received no harm from them, he was compelled to enter the temple of idols to offer sacrifice. But he said to the emperor: "Let us go to the temple of the idols." Hearing this, the emperor rejoiced and went with all the people. But the holy man prayed, and by praying, he overthrew and shattered the idols. Then the emperor, indignant, commanded that he be beheaded; and thus he yielded up his spirit to the Lord, for whom he had alacritously undergone martyrdom.
On the same day: Commemoration of the holy prophet Haggai.
This man was of the tribe of Levi; he was born in Babylon during the captivity, when the Jews were led away captives by the Persians to the city of Babylon itself, God having permitted this on account of the sins which that people had committed against Him. From his early youth, he came from Babylon to Jerusalem and prophesied together with Zechariah for thirty-six years. Both these prophets—namely, Haggai and Zechariah—preceded the incarnation of Christ by four hundred and seventy years. Haggai indeed very openly prophesied concerning the return of the people, and he himself saw the foundations of the Jerusalem temple already laid. And after having prophesied many other things, he died and was buried near the tomb of the priests with pomp and honor, just as they were, since he himself was also descended from the priestly line. Haggai was white-haired, with a round chin, and because of his virtues he was held in the highest esteem by all as an eminent and great prophet of God.
On the same day: Commemoration of the blessed Theophano, wife of Leo, our most illustrious and wisest emperor.
The blessed Lady Theophano was a native of Constantinople, an alumna of the city, and an empress. She was the daughter of Constantine, an illustrious man related to three emperors; her mother was named Anna, and she herself was also barren and infertile, just as that former Anna, the mother of the prophet Samuel. But by constantly praying to our holy Lady, the Theotokos, along with her husband—who is honored in the church of Bassus—she conceived the holy Theophano. Once born, she raised her virtuously, teaching her letters. And when the famous emperor Basil went looking for a wife for his son, the wisest emperor Leo, finding none better than her, he gave her to him in marriage. Being crowned and declared empress, she nevertheless did not abandon her care for the poor, but, retaining her accustomed virtues and zeal for almsgiving, she served God, from whom she obtained the kingdom of heaven.
In the same month: On Sunday, when the commemoration of the holy Fathers Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob occurs.
Regarding these holy Fathers of ours, the great Moses wrote accurately in the book of Genesis. We, however, will say a few things about them from the same book. Abraham was from Chaldea, most skilled in astronomy, born to an idolatrous father. But when he perceived that there is no divinity in idols, and for that reason had burned the images, he heard the true God saying to him: "Abraham, get thee out of thy country, and from thy kindred, and from thy father's house, and go to the land that I will show thee." He, therefore, having immediately bid farewell to all things, and having taken his wife Sarah, and Lot his brother's
son, he set out following God, looking to Him as he was able, and obeying His commandments. In his old age, he begat Isaac, whom God, tempting him, commanded to sacrifice; but seeing him obedient, He bade him abstain from the slaughter of Isaac. Isaac, moreover, begat Jacob, whose memory we celebrate before the birth of Christ, because Christ was born of them according to the flesh.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The seventeenth day
 A 36 The struggle of the three holy children, Ananias, Azarias, and Misael.
The three holy children were from the city of Jerusalem, sons of Ezechias, the king of the Jews—he who dared to say to God that he had kept those things which were pleasing in His sight—and when he was sick unto death, and did penance, he obtained the prolongation of his life by fifteen years. They therefore, when Jerusalem had been taken by Nabuchodonosor, the king of the Assyrians, were led captive to Babylon, where, on account of their virtue, they became princes of that region. But later, refusing to adore the golden statue which the king had made, they were thrown into a furnace, whom an angel descending preserved unharmed. Wherefore the king honored the boys and confessed the God of heaven to be great. But when another king had succeeded, seeing the three holy children believing in God, he ordered them to be beheaded; and as they were being beheaded, one received the head of the other, and at last Daniel received the severed head of Ananias: and thus they ended their lives.
On the same day
37 The struggle of Saint Daniel the prophet.
This holy Daniel, having suffered the same fortune of captivity with the three children, went from Jerusalem to Babylon. He prophesied for sixty years, and preceded the birth of Christ by four hundred and sixty years; a man continent and fasting, and tasting no food except legumes. Wherefore he conciliated to himself the love of God, from whom an angel was sent and taught him hidden mysteries. He therefore solved and interpreted the dreams of the king, and prophesied concerning the kingdoms of each nation—to wit, the Medes, Assyrians, Persians, Macedonians, and Romans—and that all kingdoms were to be dissolved, and that the empire of the Romans and Christians would prevail, with Christ reigning forever. Being cast into the lions' den, he emerged therefrom unharmed, and was honored as a son by King Balthasar. Finally, he was beheaded by King Atticus along with the three children.
On the same day
38 The struggle of Saint Bacchus the Younger, the martyr.
This man was from the region of Palestine, in the reign of Irene and Constantine, having Christian parents and ancestors, but his father, being led astray by demons, passed over to the impure sect of the Saracens, and Ha-
became addicted to the Saracen superstition. He died having seven children, whom he had also imbued with the same perverse faith, leaving them with their mother. Of these, one, called Gelasius, went to Jerusalem, sought the Laura of Saint Sabas, and became a monk, taking the name of Bacchus. Having lived a holy life, he was sent by the superior of the monastery, so that the Saracens would not know that he had baptized him. Returning home, he baptized his five brothers. But the one who had refused to receive baptism denounced Bacchus to the Ameras. The Ameras ordered him to be beheaded, and thus he departed.
ON THE SAME MONTH, THE EIGHTEENTH DAY
Commemoration of our holy Father and Prelate Florus.
In the times of the emperors Justin the Younger, Tiberius, and Maurice, this holy Florus flourished, born of a father also named Florus and a mother named Euphemia, by whom he was instructed in the learning of both the Greeks and the Christians. He became a notary to the emperors, and was afterwards enrolled in the order of the patricians; he took a wife and procreated children. But when his wife and children were consumed by the plague, he became sorrowful, and seizing the opportunity to provide for his own salvation, he retired to a certain field of his which was at the Anaplus. Afterwards, on account of his sanctity of life, he was ordained bishop of Amisus. When he had rightly governed the flock entrusted to him there, and had taught the people the best morals and the true faith, and had urged them to fulfill the mandates of God, he fell asleep, with miracles flowing from his sepulcher to this very day.
ON THE SAME MONTH, THE NINETEENTH DAY
Contest of the holy martyrs Promus, Areus, and Elias.
These men were by race Egyptians, but by profession Christians. Inflamed with divine zeal, they used to visit the holy martyrs who were detained in prisons for the sake of Christ, and they provided them with the things necessary to sustain life. Having therefore set out for Cilicia, intending afterwards to enter the city of Ascalon, they were seized at the gate by the guards, a barbarous and rustic race of men, as if they were traitors. Being brought to the governor Firmilianus and questioned by him, they professed themselves to be Christians. Wherefore they were most cruelly beaten with scourges; and Areus indeed, having been first tormented with numberless tortures, finally died delivered to the fire. Saint Promus, however, having been tested with many trials, and remaining always constant in the faith of Christ, was beheaded. Finally, Saint Elias, having been tortured in many ways, was himself also struck down by the sword and beheaded.
On the same day.
Contest of the holy martyr Timotheus the deacon.
Timotheus the holy martyr was in the region of Mauritania
and was ordained deacon of the church in that region by the bishop of the same place. As he was teaching the people the faith in Christ and the kingdom of the heavens, and on account of this was baptizing many, and refuting the error of the Greeks as one of falsity, he was seized by the governor of the region, who was an idolater. And having been greatly punished and tortured, and locked in prisons, and distressed by hunger and thirst, and not being persuaded to renounce Christ and sacrifice to the impure demons, at last, a great pyre being made, he was cast into it. There, entering in, he was chanting, praying, and gave up his spirit to the Lord. His precious remaining relics were deposited in that same region of Mauritania by faithful and pious men, to the glory of Christ our God.
Eodem die. Contest of the holy martyr Polyeuctus of Caesarea. The martyr Polyeuctus shone at Caesarea. Because he was a Christian and preached Christ, imbueing everyone with His faith, and was bringing many of the Greeks from error to piety, he was denounced to the prefect of Caesarea; and by soldiers sent by him, he was seized and thrust into custody, with an order added to the jailer that he should guard him strictly. When, however, the prefect was free from public business, he sent soldiers who brought the saint out of prison and set him before his tribunal. Approaching him to induce him to renounce Christ and sacrifice to idols, when he had wasted his efforts, he first commanded his whole body to be flayed with iron claws, then the teeth to be pulled out; finally, he commanded him to be cast into a great burning furnace, where, rejoicing and glad, he gave up his spirit to the Lord.
EODEM MENSE. The twentieth day.
Contest of the most holy martyr Ignatius, patriarch of great Antioch.
The emperor Trajan was setting out on an expedition against the Parthians. And passing through Antioch of Syria, he learned concerning the holy Ignatius that he was making many of the Greeks Christians. He sent therefore, and immediately seized him, and locked him in prison. Then, bringing him out of custody, he commanded that he be taken to Rome, so that he might be devoured by beasts. On this account he was handed over to a decury of soldiers to be brought to Rome; by whom on the journey he was very poorly treated. Finally, he was brought to Rome and set before the Senate. But the Senate, following the emperor's command, condemned him to the beasts. But the saint had desired this same thing, beseeching God that he might be devoured by beasts and thus appear as pure bread to God; which also came to pass. Ignatius was a successor of the holy apostles and a disciple of holy John the Theologian.
On the same day. A 
The Contest of the holy martyrs Eugenius and Macarius.
In the reign of Julian the Apostate, these holy men came and stood before him, and said to him: "O most impious and lawless one, why have you abandoned the true God and joined yourself to unclean demons?" Enraged by these words, he ordered them to be cast into a pit, which was full of many reptiles, snakes and worms, and other kinds of wild beasts. But when they escaped from them unharmed, he sent them in bonds into the region of Mauritania, to a city called Andedon. There, having enlightened the people who were idolaters and performed various miracles, and finally having killed, through prayer, the dragon that dwelt on the mountain and injured many, they persuaded many to believe in Christ. Afterwards, living in comfort, they surrendered their souls in peace at different times, receiving the crown for their confession of Christ.
On the same day. B 
The Contest of the holy martyr Boniface.
In the reign of Diocletian, he was the servant of a certain woman named Aglais, the daughter of Acacius, the proconsul of Rome. Although he was a slave, he was in the habit of committing physical defilement with his mistress. Yet both were of a very generous mind towards the poor; and Aglais, in particular, pursued the martyrs with the highest love. One day, therefore, she said to Boniface that, if he were willing, he should go to the East, where the holy men were undergoing martyrdom, and bring back the relics of the saints, so that she might have them for the salvation of her soul. Boniface said to her: "What if I should bring back my own relics?" She laughed at these words, and calling him a drunkard, sent him away. He therefore, having gone with twelve servants and much gold to Cilicia, where the saints were being tortured, voluntarily presented himself to the prefect and confessed Christ. Wherefore, having been most bitterly tortured, he was beheaded. His relics, having been brought back by his fellow servants and handed over to his mistress, were honorably buried by her.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
On the twenty-first day. D 
The Contest of the holy martyr Juliana.
The martyr Juliana lived during the reign of the Emperor Maximian, and was from the city of Nicomedia. As her parents were wealthy, she was betrothed to Eleusius, a senator, who wished to celebrate the marriage. But the holy woman did not consent, saying, "Unless he becomes the prefect of the city, I will not take him as a husband." And when this had also come to pass, she said again, "Unless he becomes a Christian, I will not take him." Hearing these things, her father was angered and tried the more to persuade her to deny Christ and marry her husband. As he did not persuade her, he then delivered her, in accordance with the laws, to her betrothed and the prefect for examination and torture. The prefect, therefore, ordered her whole body to be suspended by the hair and torn, and her skin
ripped from her skull. Then he cast her into the fire, and finally beheaded her, along with others who had believed because of her.
On the same day.
The Passion of the Holy Martyr Themistocles
This holy Themistocles lived under the reign of Decius and was from the region of Lycia. He was tending his sheep on the mountain where the holy Dioscorides was hiding to avoid the persecution. Soldiers sent by the regional prefect to arrest holy Dioscorides, not having found him, came upon Themistocles tending his sheep and questioned him about Dioscorides. Although he knew well where he was hiding, he would not betray him, but surrendered himself in his stead, confessing himself a Christian. Wherefore, he was bound in chains and brought before the prefect; and having confessed Christ before him, he was stretched out face upward on the ground and beaten on his belly for so long that his entrails burst forth and he was split in two. Then, he was put upon the rack, and after being taken down, was dragged over iron caltrops, and thus he surrendered his spirit to the Lord.
On the same day.
Commemoration of our Holy Father Philogonius, Lawyer turned Bishop
This holy Philogonius was a cultivator of piety from infancy, and when he reached adulthood, he took a wife and sired a child. He became an advocate, and if he saw anyone oppressed unjustly and suffering, he would take up their cause and ward off the injury. When his wife died, because of his most excellent virtue and love of justice, from being an advocate he was ordained bishop by the suffrages of all the people and the bishops, and by the splendor of his virtues and all his righteous deeds, he surpassed the light of the sun. He was also admirable in wisdom and doctrine. For there was much corruption of morals at that time, as the persecution had recently ceased and heresy was taking hold. But by the grace of God, he suppressed this heresy. And having shepherded in the best manner the flock entrusted to him, and having led an angelic life upon earth, he rested in peace, delivering his soul to the Lord.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The twenty-second day.
The Passion of the Holy Martyr Anastasia and the holy women with her
The martyr Anastasia flourished under the Emperor Diocletian in the city of Rome, born of wealthy and noble parents. She was taught the faith of Christ by her mother, and was instructed in the sacred scriptures by Chrysogonus, a teacher of Christians. She had taken a husband, Publius by name, who was wealthy indeed, but a pagan. Because he was not a Christian, Anastasia hated him and would not grant him any power over her body; she was, however, kind and generous to the poor and to those who were detained in prison.
For this reason she was cast into prison by her husband. Yet when he died, she was released from prison, gave all of her wealth to the poor, and again served the saints, buried those who had been perfected, and encouraged many to endure martyrdom. Having been tortured by various prefects, and cast into the sea, she was then handed over to the fire. Emerging from the fire, she was beheaded, along with the women who were with her.
The same day.
Commemoration of the holy hieromartyr Capiton, Bishop of Cherson.
He was sent as bishop to Cherson during the reign of Theodosius the Great, after the death of the bishop Etherius. Upon arriving in that city, he began to teach the people the faith in Christ. There was in the city of Cherson a temple called the idol of Parthenius. Wishing to destroy it and to build in its place a church in the name of the holy apostle Peter, he kindled furnaces to prepare materials for the construction. The Greeks and the Jews, seized with fury, after long opposing the saint as the destroyer of their gods, finally came to him and said: "If you enter one of the furnaces and emerge unharmed, not only will we all believe, but we will also cast our own children into the furnace." The bishop entered the furnace and came out unharmed. Seeing this, all the idolaters believed in Christ, and then they destroyed the temple of the idols. After the saint had performed many other deeds, he departed in peace to the Lord.
The same day.
The struggle of the holy martyr Chrysogonus.
Chrysogonus the martyr was from the great city of Rome during the reign of the emperor Diocletian; he was a pious man who feared God, having become a teacher of the faith in Christ and of the holy scriptures to the holy martyr Anastasia. When a persecution was stirred up, he was seized and cast into prison. It was then that St. Anastasia wrote to him, that he might pray for her, and that if her husband should become a Christian, he might live; but if he would not be persuaded, that he might die, so that he would not squander her wealth with idolaters, but rather that she might exhaust it upon the saints and the poor. Which indeed happened: for once the saint had obtained her freedom, she gave everything she possessed to the poor, and having put on a lowly garment, she went about serving the saints who were held in the prisons. At that time Diocletian, residing in Nicaea, wrote to Rome that all Christians should be put to death, and that Chrysogonus should be brought to him in chains at Nicaea; and when he was brought, he ordered that he be beheaded.
The same day.
The struggle of the holy women Irene, Agape, and Chionia.
At the time when Chrysogonus the martyr, by the order of the emperor Diocletian, was beheaded for the confession of Christ near the lake, beside which lived
the three holy sisters, Agape, Chionia, and Irene, along with a certain Zoilus, a servant of God, the same Chrysogonus appeared to Zoilus in his sleep, saying to him that St. Anastasia was about to join in the contest with the three holy sisters, who were due to complete the struggle of their martyrdom within nine days. When St. Anastasia heard this, she followed St. Chrysogonus, and went to the holy women, and embraced them, and served them. When Diocletian learned of this, he seized them and delivered them to the prefect of the region; and having been tortured by him, they were handed over again to another prefect, named Sisinius. The prefect ordered St. Agape and St. Chionia to be consumed by fire, and St. Irene was killed by a soldier with a sword.
On the same day: The contest of the holy martyr Theodota and her children.
The martyr Theodota was born in Bithynia; moved by the celebrated fame of Anastasia, she went to her, and lived with her, along with her children. Prior to this, the saint herself was sought in marriage by the prefect Leucadius, and she refused him; but wishing to escape him, she asked him to wait a little while, saying that she would soon comply with his will. While the prefect was waiting, she distributed her wealth to the poor and ministered to the saints. When Diocletian learned that the prisons and dungeons were full of Christians, he gave orders that they all be put to death in a single night, some by fire, others by water, and others by the sword. Therefore, Leucadius handed over St. Theodota and her children to Nicetius, the prefect of Bithynia, that he might torture them. When he was rebuked by Evodus, the firstborn son of Theodota, he flared up in rage, ordered a huge furnace to be lit, and cast Theodota and her children into it.
In the same month, the twenty-third day: The contest of the ten holy martyrs in Crete.
These were in the time of the emperor Decius, from the island of Crete, yet not from the same city, but from various parts of the region. From the metropolis of Gortyna came five: Theodulus, Saturninus, Euporus, Gelasius, and Eunicianus; from Cnossus came Zoticus; from Epinium, Leomenes and Pontius; from Panormus, Agathopus; from Cydonia, Basilides; and from Heraclea, Evarestus. Having been seized by the unbelievers, they were handed over to the prefect of the island. He commanded the populace to lead them in chains to the altars of the idols, and if they did not sacrifice, to torture them most severely, as each saw fit. For the space of thirty days they were led around, mocked, beaten, stoned, and dragged through manure; finally, the prefect sitting at the tribunal, they were beheaded before him, thanking God to their very last breath.
ON THE SAME MONTH, THE TWENTY-FOURTH DAY
Contest of Saint Eugenia the Martyr, and her companions.
In the reign of Commodus, there was Saint Eugenia, daughter of Philip, the prefect of Rome. When her father was sent to Alexandria by Commodus to be prefect of that city, and took his wife and children with him, she too went along, and there she learned all Greek and Christian literature. When she searched the Scriptures, she came upon the Epistles of the holy Apostle Paul; embracing the Christian faith through reading them, she gave herself over to the practice of all virtues. Then, abandoning her ancestral splendor and assuming male attire, she withdrew to a monastery, and there was baptized and took up the monastic life, having claimed to be a eunuch. In time, having been recognized and identified by her father Philip, she led him, as well as her mother and relatives, to the faith of Christ. Shortly afterward, her father became a bishop, and subsequently they all received the end through martyrdom.
ON THE SAME MONTH, THE TWENTY-FIFTH DAY
Commemoration of the immaculate Nativity of our Lord Jesus Christ.
The most merciful God, seeing the human race oppressed by the tyranny of the devil, had compassion on it; and having sent His angel Gabriel, he said to the Theotokos: "Hail, full of grace, the Lord is with you." And immediately our Lord and God was conceived in her immaculate womb. When nine months had passed since the conception, a decree went out from Caesar Augustus that the whole world should be registered. And Quirinius was sent to Jerusalem, to Bethlehem, to conduct the census. Joseph also, the guardian of the Virgin, went up with her to Bethlehem to be registered. And since the Virgin was about to give birth, and finding no room in the inn because of the overflowing crowd, she entered a humble cave; and there she incorruptibly gave birth to our Lord Jesus Christ, and swaddled as a babe the Creator of all, and placed Him in a manger of irrational animals, as one who was to deliver us from irrationality.
ON THE SAME DAY
The Adoration of the Magi.
In those days there was a seer named Balaam in the land of the Persians, who, having prophesied many other things, also foretold this: "A star shall rise out of Jacob, and it shall crush the sons of Moab." The other seers and magi, therefore, receiving such a prophecy by tradition, taught it to all the seers of the Persians; and it came down to the Magi and kings of the Persians who lived after them—three in number—who were diligently watching for the appearance of such a star. As skilled astronomers, when they saw the star of Christ, seeing that it did not follow the course of the other stars, moving from east to west, but rather move toward the south, they recognized that it signified the birth of the great King, and following...
the star, they found Christ the Lord. And falling down, they worshipped Him, and offered Him gifts, gold, frankincense, and myrrh; and thus, by the command of the angel, they returned with joy into their own country.
On the same day, Joseph the betrothed and guardian of the Virgin is warned by the divine angel.
When Herod, the king of the Jews, had learned from the Magi that a great King had been born, who was to subdue the whole world, he called the Magi and said, "Go and search diligently for the child; and when you have found him, tell me, so that I also may come and worship him."
