Our holy and great Father Bendimianus was a disciple of our holy Father Auxentius, who lived a solitary life on the mountain near Chalcedon, called Oxia; he showed himself a diligent servant to his master, as is fitting for the saints, and becoming heir to his holy prayers, he devoted his efforts to acquiring virtues: and not content with these, after the death of the saint, having found a crag near the saint's monastery, amid the mountain defiles, he built a small cell upon it on two sides, and enclosing himself therein, he struggled for forty-two years. He also received the gift of healings, and healed those coming to him of both mental and bodily diseases. Furthermore, he endured many temptations from demons. And being forewarned of his own death, he narrated the life of holy Auxentius to a certain writer so that he might commit it to letters; and thus, bowing his knees, he gave up the spirit.
IN THE SAME MONTH. The Second Day.
A 154 The Meeting of our Lord Jesus Christ, when the elder Simeon received him in his arms.
Our Lord Jesus Christ, in order to confirm his economy according to the flesh—that is, that he was truly made man—having fulfilled all things which were ordained by the Jewish law, did not omit this either, but did as was the custom among the Jews. For on the fortieth day, the parents used to bring every Hebrew male child into the temple, along with two turtledoves or two young pigeons, so that he might receive a blessing. Our Lord Jesus Christ also did this. And along with his mother, the Theotokos, who is truly his mother, and Joseph, his putative father, he came into the temple of God, and was received by the just Simeon, who, when he had taken him into his arms, said: "Now thou dost dismiss thy servant, O Lord, according to thy word in peace, because my eyes have seen thy salvation." Likewise, Anna the prophetess also confessed and gave thanks to God, because he had visited his people. For this reason, therefore, this holy feast is celebrated.
On the same day
155 The contest of the holy martyrs Perpetua, Satyrus, Revocatus, Saturninus, Secundus, and Felicitas.
Perpetua, a martyr of Christ from the region of Africa, having been arrested by the heathens on account of her confession of Christ, was led to the provincial prefect along with the rest of the holy ones. Now the holy woman had an infant who was still nursing, and a brother named Dinocrates. Therefore, after they were tortured by the prefect, they were cast into custody, where the holy woman saw in a dream a bronze ladder fixed to the earth and reaching to the heavens, and at its extremities were fixed all kinds of instruments of torture, while at its base a dragon was attempting to pull down those who were ascending. But she saw the holy Satyrus ascending, and turning to her, he said, "My mother Perpetua, I am waiting for you." Now her brother, who was enclosed in another prison, saw Satyrus standing at a basin of water and scooping from it and drinking, from which he understood that he was dead. In the morning, therefore, Perpetua and Felicitas were gored by the horns of a ferocious bull and met their end; but the others were slaughtered. D
IN THE SAME MONTH. The Third Day.
156 Commemoration of the holy and just Simeon, who received the Lord in his arms, and of Anna the prophetess.
Simeon the elder, just and pious, and abstaining himself from all evil, while he frequented the temple and prayed to God that he might have mercy on the world and free mankind from the devil, heard from an angel that he would not die before he saw the Christ of the Lord, who would bring salvation to the whole world. Likewise, Anna the pro-
prophetess praying, also received such an oracle. And when forty days had been completed from the birth of Christ, his parents brought the child Jesus into the temple, according to the ordinance of the law. And Simeon, having taken him into his arms, said: Now you dismiss your servant, O Lord, according to your word, in peace, because my eyes have seen your salvation. And having prophesied concerning the death of Christ, and concerning the cross, and the Theotokos—that she would be grieved seeing Jesus hanging on the cross and would be consumed with sorrow—he fell asleep. Similarly, Anna also, having given thanks to God, reached the end of her life.
On the same day: The contest of the holy martyrs Adrian and Eubulus, who suffered martyrdom in Caesarea.
Adrian and Eubulus, martyrs of Christ, were from a region called Banea. As Christians and pious men, they had an ardent desire toward the martyrs and the confessors of Christ. For this reason they went to Caesarea, that they might visit the saints being tormented in the prisons. And when they arrived, they visited them, and comforted and encouraged them. But they also taught the Gentiles the word of Christ, and baptized many of them. And having been informed upon and arrested, they were delivered to Firmilian the prefect. And they were beaten severely; and their sides were burned with fire. Then lions were let loose upon them, but they did not harm them; but the lions went and, prostrating themselves before them, licked their feet. Then the prefect, having become angered and boiling over with rage, beheaded them; and thus they were perfected.
On the same day: The contest of the holy martyrs Papias, Diodorus, and Claudianus.
Papias, Diodorus, and Claudianus, the venerable martyrs of Christ, lived during the time of Decius the emperor, from the city of Attalia in the province of Pamphylia. And while tending various animals in their own country, they were nonetheless Christians, and they taught the Gentiles about Christ, and they converted many to Him. And having been accused, they were arrested by Publius, the governor of Pamphylia. And being questioned, they confessed with boldness the name of Christ, of our true God, and taught that He Himself is the one who made the heaven, and the earth, and the sea, and all things that are in them, and that beside Him there is no other God. Having heard these things, the governor, having become angry, punished them severely. But since he could not induce them to deny Christ, he ordered their heads to be cut off by the sword; and thus they were perfected, and were buried by the Christians honorably, to the glory of Christ our God.
On the same day: Commemoration of our holy Father Isidore of Pelusium.
This holy man was an Egyptian from the city of Pelusium, of noble
and pious parents; he was a kinsman of Theophilus and Cyril, bishops of Alexandria, and was excellently instructed in sacred and secular literature. But, preferring the love of God to the age, he renounced his parents, riches, and glory, and withdrew to a mountain not far from Pelusium. There he became a monk, and while leading a tranquil life, he devoted all his efforts now to prayer, and now to reflection upon books, illuminating the entire world with his epistles, and explaining all the holy Scriptures to them. And when he had guided many and rendered them clear in virtues (for he did not only benefit private individuals with the doctrines of his epistles, but also princes, and bishops, and emperors; for he wrote ten thousand epistles), having lived a most holy life, he fell asleep.
On the same day: The Contest of the holy hieromartyr Abramius the Persian
Abramius the hieromartyr was bishop of a certain city in Persis, and under Sapor, the tyrant of the Persians rather than their king, he was accused by the Magi, who worshipped fire and the sun, because he believed in Christ and worshipped Him. He was seized, bound in iron chains, and brought to the prince of the Magi; by whom he was compelled to abjure Christ and to worship the creatures, namely the sun and fire (for the deluded Persians held these in the place of gods). When he could not be induced to do this, he was subjected to countless torments, at each of which he would invoke Christ and say: "Lord Jesus Christ, O God, help me, Thy servant, for in Thee does my soul trust." Now after he had been snatched from the torments, he was thrust into prison; from where, being led out again and tortured, he finally was beheaded, giving thanks to God.
IN THE SAME MONTH. The Fifth Day. The Contest of the holy martyr Agatha
Agatha, the martyr of Christ, was born in Panormus in Sicily, and was renowned for her beauty, piety, wealth, and fear of God. During the times of the impious Emperor Decius, she was seized and brought to the prefect of the province and handed over to a woman named Aphrodisia, that she might be enticed by her seductions to abjure Christ and worship idols. But when she could not be persuaded to this, but rather longed for death for the sake of Christ, she was most severely beaten, and one of her breasts was cut off. But having been cast into prison, the holy apostle Peter appeared to her by night and, by the grace of God, restored her amputated breast whole. Then, being led out of the prison, she was dragged over broken pottery and burned with fire; and having been delivered from all these by the virtue of Christ, she was again shut up in custody; where, giving thanks to God and praying, she departed this life.
MONTH OF THE SAME. Seventh Day.
The Contest of the Holy Martyr Julian of Emesa.
This man was from the city of Emesa. Being a young man, he learned the medical art and would treat and heal the sick. Using the pretext of healing, he taught them the faith in Christ and baptized them. One day, when Silvanus the bishop, Luke the deacon, and Mocius the reader had been seized by the Greeks to be tortured, during the times of the Emperor Numerian, and had been locked up in prison and sentenced to be devoured by beasts, the holy Julian saw what was happening and, catching their zeal, was distressed. When he saw them being led out of prison, bound and dragged toward the beasts, he ran to them and, with great boldness, kissed them and prayed for them. Then he too was seized and bound with them; and having been nailed through the head, hands, and feet, he entered a certain cave and died.
On the same day, the Contest of the holy martyr Fausta and those with her, Evilasius and Maximus.
Fausta, the martyr of Christ, lived under the Emperor Maximian, being the daughter of rich and pious parents from the city of Cyzicus. But after they died, although she was young and wealthy, she did not love the pleasures of the world; rather, she passed her days in virginity, fasting, vigils, and almsgiving to the poor, and she served God. She was also intent upon the reading of the Holy Scriptures. Because of this, her fame reached the Emperor, and Evilasius the governor was sent to her, to persuade her to sacrifice to the idols. But when he saw the miracles wrought by her, he believed in Christ. Then Maximus the prefect was sent to torture Fausta and Evilasius, and he made a bronze vessel boil and threw the saints into it; but when he saw that they were not burned, he himself, having believed in Christ, voluntarily cast himself into the vessel; and thus all three were perfected.
On the same day, the Contest of the holy Silvanus the bishop, Luke the deacon, and Mocius the reader.
These saints lived during the reign of Numerian, from the region of the Emesenes. Silvanus was the bishop of that city, Luke was deacon, and Mocius was reader. As they were teaching the word of God, and confessing Christ as the Son of God and true God, they were seized by the Greeks and delivered to the governor. Having been interrogated and having confessed before him the name of our Master Jesus Christ, they were severely tortured. Soon after the sentence was passed that they should be delivered to the beasts to be torn apart, as they were being led to punishment, the holy Julian—who had been baptized by their agency—ran up, kissed them, and asked that he might fulfill his martyrdom together with them. But the saints, in the theater...
when the wild beasts were set upon them, giving thanks to God, they finished their lives.
Eodem die
166 Commemoration of the holy Bishop Faustus.
Missing.
Eodem die
167 Commemoration of our holy Father Bucolus, Bishop of Smyrna, disciple of John the Theologian.
Our holy Father Bucolus, from his early youth, applied all his effort to being pious and fearing God. When the holy John the Theologian recognized this zeal, he took him and made him his disciple; and he stayed with him while he was in Ephesus. Seeing him endowed with virtue, shining with all spiritual works, and perfect, he made him Bishop of Smyrna. Led by the Holy Spirit, he converted many of the infidels from error, brought them to the one God, and baptized them. He excelled to such a degree that he also prophesied future events; wherefore, foreseeing by the prophetic spirit that Saint Polycarp would succeed him, while he was still alive he ordained him, and established him as bishop and pastor in his place. Having died and been buried, he caused a plant to sprout from his tomb, which effects many cures.
OF THE SAME MONTH
The seventh day.
Conflict of the three thousand holy martyrs who suffered martyrdom at Nicomedia.
These were subjects of the four protectors who, sent by the Emperor Diocletian, had apprehended Saint Peter, Archbishop of Alexandria, and beheaded him. Being then converted to the faith of Christ together with all their own people, after the death of Saint Peter they themselves also were slain for Christ. Seeing their masters die for Christ, they were saddened, and being kindled with zeal, they took their wives and children and all their kinsmen, and came to Nicomedia, crying out and saying before the emperor: "We also are Christians." When Diocletian heard this, being troubled and inflamed with anger, he first attempted to induce them by blandishments to deny Christ. But when he had accomplished nothing, he commanded the soldiers to cut them down with swords before him.
Eodem die
169 Commemoration of the holy Parthenius, Bishop of Lampsacus.
Our holy Father Parthenius, under the Emperor Constantine the Great, was the son of Christopher, a deacon of the church of Melitopolis; he was indeed unskilled in letters, but was endowed with the highest piety and virtue. While leading the life of a fisherman, he was selling the fish he had caught...
to those who sought them. And so great was the virtue hidden within him, that he merited receiving from God the gift of casting out demons and healing diseases. And when he later learned letters, he was ordained presbyter by Philip, the bishop of Melitopolis, and then bishop of Lampsacus by Ascholius, the metropolitan of Cyzicus. He performed many miracles: for he restored the eye of a certain man that had been gouged out by the horn of a bull; he healed the ailment which they call cancer; and he recalled to life a man who had been trampled under a wagon. And having performed many other marvels, and having foretold things to come, he departed in peace.
On the same day
Commemoration of Saint Arion, Bishop of Cyprus. B 
Missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The eighth day
Commemoration of the holy and glorious prophet Zechariah, one of the sixteen prophets.
This prophet was born in the region of Judaea, but he came to the land of the Chaldaeans already at an advanced age, and there he foretold many things, and gave signs and portents in demonstration of those things which he prophesied. He said to the prince Josedech that he would receive a son, who would serve in the priesthood at Jerusalem. Likewise, he blessed Salathiel in his son, upon whom he also bestowed the name Zorobabel. Concerning King Cyrus, he also gave a sign of prophecy for victory; and he prophesied concerning his own ministry which he would perform in Jerusalem; and finally concerning the end of the nations, and concerning the prophecies and priesthood of the Jews, that they would come to an end; and concerning the temple, that it would be destroyed; and concerning the advent of Christ, that He would utterly destroy all idolatry and rule forever. Having prophesied these things and many others, he died in deep old age and was honorably buried near the tomb of the prophet Haggai.
On the same day
The contest of the holy great martyr Theodore the Stratelates who was in Euchaita.
The great martyr Theodore the Stratelates flourished under the Emperor Licinius: he had his home in Euchaita, but he spent his time in Heraclea of Pontus, a man so distinguished in eloquence, beauty, and prudence, that the emperor burned with a desire to see him: for he knew that he was handsome and brave, but yet a worshipper of Christ. Therefore, he sent men to summon him to Nicomedia. But he said to the messengers: "Nay, but rather," he says, "let the emperor come here, and let him bring with him his golden and silver gods, to whom he commands sacrifices to be offered." When Licinius learned this, he was filled with joy and did so. Theodore, however, upon learning of the arrival of Licinius, rode out on horseback to meet him, and honored him with such honor as was fitting for an emperor. The emperor, therefore, having entered the city, urged the saint to offer sacrifices to the gods; and he requested to take them and
attend to them first at home, and having taken them, he crushed them and gave them to the poor. Because of this, after being tortured at length, he was later beheaded; and his precious remains were brought back and placed in Euchaita.
Eodem die
Commemoration of our holy Father Theodosius.
Our most holy Father Theodosius was born in Antioch of Syria, the son of noble and wealthy parents. But abandoning parents, and glory, and wealth, and the world, he departed to a mountain situated in Cilicia; and having found a rock hollowed into a small shelter, sloping toward the sea, he dwelt there. Then, building a small hut as well, he remained there, giving himself over to fasts, and vigils, and prayers, and consuming his body. His garment was of rough hair: but he also carried iron plates on his neck, his hands, and in the middle of his body. Having become such and so great, he obtained favor from God, and boldness before Him, and performed many miracles: for he made water gush forth from a dry rock, and saved many who were in danger at sea. Finally, having gone to Antioch on account of the incursion of the Isaurians, he attained his end.
Eodem die
The struggle of the holy martyrs Martha and Mary, own sisters, and of Lycarion the monk.
The holy martyr of Christ, Martha, and her sister Mary, were virgins who were pious and God-fearing. When, however, the prefect of the province was passing by the house where they were staying, they looked out from the window and professed themselves to be Christians. But the prefect, observing their youthful age, said: "I would wish you to be saved entirely; but unless you sacrifice to the gods, you will perish miserably." The saints replied to him: "It is not death to die for Christ, but life." The same was said by Lycarion, a young monk who struggled in virtue with the virgins, and he added that the gods of the Greeks were empty idols, and their worshippers miserable and unhappy. The prefect, therefore, enraged, ordered the soldiers to kill them: who indeed put the saints to the cross and pierced them with swords; but the monk Lycarion they beheaded. And in this manner, having completed their life, they surrendered their souls to the Lord.
Eodem die
The struggle of the holy martyr Philadelphus. 
Missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH. 
On the ninth day.
The struggle of the holy martyr Nicephorus.
The glorious martyr of Christ, Nicephorus, lived in the time of the emperors Valerian and Gallienus, while he was living a private life. He was on familiar terms with a certain Sapricius,
a presbyter of the Christians, who later, at the devil's instigation, hated the holy Nicephorus and would not be reconciled to him by any means. When persecution was stirred up and Sapricius was seized by the idolaters and compelled to sacrifice to the idols, holy Nicephorus sent mediators to him, begging that he might forgive him before he underwent martyrdom. But Sapricius would not grant him pardon. And when he saw him being dragged by the executioner to be beheaded, he ran to him and, throwing himself at the feet of Sapricius, entreated him again and again to forgive him. But not even in this way could he move the wretched man. Wherefore, rejected by God, when he already had his neck stretched out and saw the executioner's sword drawn, he turned to the officers and said: "Let me go, and I will sacrifice to the idols." Then holy Nicephorus ran up and put his own neck in the place of Sapricius, and was beheaded for Christ.
On the same day. 
Commemoration of Marcianus, bishop of Sicily, Philagrius, bishop of Cyprus, and Pancratius, bishop of Tauromenium.
These were disciples of the holy apostle Peter. For the father of Saint Pancratius, while Christ was still living in the body on earth and working miracles, was moved by the report of him and went from the city of Antioch to Jerusalem with Pancratius, in order to see him. And having become an acquaintance of the holy Peter after Christ's ascension, he followed him, and by him was ordained bishop of Tauromenium; and while he was teaching in the name of Christ, he was killed secretly by the Gentiles. Likewise, Marcianus, having been made bishop of Sicily, after he had converted many of the unbelievers to the Lord, departed this life. Philagrius, however, having been appointed bishop of Cyprus, while he also was teaching in the name of Christ, having suffered many afflictions from demons and from impious and unbelieving men, died, giving thanks to God until his very last breath.
OF THE SAME MONTH. 
The tenth day.
Contest of the holy Charalampus, Porphyrius, Dauctus, and three women.
Charalampus was a martyr of Christ in the city of Magnesia, a priest of the Christians. While he was teaching the way of truth, he was seized by the prefect Lucianus. And he was stripped of his priestly vestment; then all the skin of his body was flayed off. And when the prefect saw him enduring these things with a constant mind, he was inflamed with anger and attempted to tear the holy man with his own hands. But immediately his hands were cut off; and the holy man, having prayed, made him sound and whole. When Porphyrius the executioner and Dauctus saw this, they abjured the superstition of idols and believed in Christ; likewise also three women who were standing there, moved by the miracle and confessing Christ, were seized and cruelly tortured. For even though the prefect was restored to health,
yet he remained in his unbelief, and with much anger and cruelty he beheaded them all.
IN THE SAME MONTH. THE ELEVENTH DAY.
The Contest of the Holy Hieromartyr Blasius, and the Holy Women with him.
This man, in the times of the Emperor Licinius, was bishop of Sebaste; and for the sake of avoiding the persecution, he hid himself in a cave, one of those in the mountains, where the wild beasts, being tamed by the blessing of the holy man, became manageable. Being skilled in the medical art, he performed many cures, receiving the grace of performing miracles from the Lord. Having been apprehended and led to the prefect Agricolaus, and having confessed the name of Christ, he was beaten with rods; then, mounted on the rack, he was torn. Later, as he was being led away to prison, seven women followed him, professing themselves to be Christians, and they were immediately beheaded. But holy Blasius, having first been thrown into the depths of a lake, yet emerging from it to the land with dry feet, was beheaded together with the two children who were in bonds with him.
IN THE SAME MONTH. THE SAME DAY.
The Finding of the Precious Relics of the Holy Prophet Zacharias.
Is missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH. THE SAME DAY.
Commemoration of Theodora Augusta, who established Orthodoxy.
Blessed Theodora Augusta was the wife of the Emperor Theophilus the Iconoclast; yet she was not a heretic, as her husband was. For he had banished the holy Methodius, the Patriarch of Constantinople, and had substituted for him John the Leconomant as patriarch, and had consumed the holy images with fire. But she, even if she did not openly dare to adore the holy images, yet secretly had them in her bedchamber, and at night she was praying and entreating God that at length He might have mercy upon the orthodox. She bore a son, Michael by name, and taught him the orthodox faith. Therefore after the death of her husband, she immediately recalled the holy Methodius, and having gathered the holy Synod, she anathematized the Iconoclasts, and cast John out from his throne, and restored the holy images to the church. Finally she died, after she had left the empire to her son Michael.
IN THE SAME MONTH. THE TWELFTH DAY.
Commemoration of our Holy Father Antony, Archbishop of Constantinople.
Antony, surnamed Blacheas, was born of pious, wealthy, and noble parents of the senatorial order; and at the age of five he was attempting to perform the duties of priests: for he did not, like children, run to the
games, though his mother was displeased at this on account of the boy’s tender age. After her death, he was surrendered by his father to a monastery and became a monk. When he had reached manhood, he was ordained a presbyter, even against his will, because of his signal virtue. Seeing such great progress in his son, his father also chose to become a monk, assuming the monastic habit from his son, so that he who was father in the flesh might be son in the spirit. He was so renowned for his beneficence toward the poor that, while he was passing by on one occasion, he received a heavy purse of gold and heard someone saying to him, "Take this and give it to the poor." He heard the voice, but did not see the person offering the gold. Being such a man as this, by the vote of the Emperor and the synod, he was made patriarch of Constantinople; and in this manner he finished his life.
Eodem die
Commemoration of Saint Maria, who, having changed her name, was called Marinus.
Saint Maria, wishing to remain hidden from men and to save her own soul, exchanged her feminine clothing and took on the garb of a man, calling herself Marinus instead of Maria. Along with her own father, Eugenius, she entered a monastery and was tonsured. There she served the monks, together with the brothers subject to the rule and her own father, while the monks were ignorant of her sex. However, when she was staying at an inn on one occasion, she was accused of having violated the innkeeper's daughter. She bore this infamy with the most equitable soul, and confessed to having committed the sin which she had not done. Wherefore, being cast out from the gates of the monastery, she spent three years there in hardships, nurturing the child, as though she were its father. Afterward, she was admitted into the monastery with the child, and soon after she departed this life, and the matter—how it had happened—was revealed; for the innkeeper's daughter, suffering from a demon, cried out that she had been defiled by a certain soldier, and not by Marinus. Therefore, all were struck with amazement and glorified God.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The thirteenth day
Commemoration of our holy Father Martinianus.
The holy Martinianus was born in Caesarea of Palestine. Having embraced the monastic life, he withdrew into the mountains; and when he had spent twenty-five years in the religious exercise, he was tempted by the devil. For a certain harlot, instigated by wicked men and dressed in vile clothing, came to him by night, pretending that she had lost her way, and began to cry out for him to open the cell and admit her, so that she might not be torn apart by wild beasts. When she had entered, wearing her harlotic clothing that night, she strove with many seductive words to soften the soul of the holy man and to lure him to foul lust. But he, before accomplishing anything, coming to his senses, lit a fire of brushwood and threw himself into the middle of the flames, saying to himself: "Unhappy Martinianus, if you are able to endure the punishment of fire, then commit the sin." And being thus burned, the shameless
woman was persuaded also to live temperately and was saved. Having fled, and having gone to a rock situated in the midst of the sea, he was tempted again; and being thus tempted and emerging victorious, he migrated to the Lord.
On the same day: The Contest of Saints Aquila and Priscilla.
Saint Aquila first practiced the tent-making craft. Though he was a pagan, he was always most favorably disposed toward the poor, so that the profit he earned from the work of his hands he distributed among the poor. When he heard the fame of the Apostle Paul preaching Christ, he went to him along with his wife Priscilla, as if to a professor of the same craft (for he himself was also expert in the tent-making trade). Being taught by him the word of truth, they were baptized and joined themselves to him as companions and servants, wherever he might journey, undergoing the same troubles and hardships with him. On this account, the great Apostle loved them vehemently for their virtue and their faith in Christ, so much so that he made mention of them in his Epistles. But after the death of the Apostle, since they did not cease to profess Christ, they were seized by the idolaters, and after many torments, they were beheaded.
On the same day: Commemoration of Saint Eulogius, Bishop of Alexandria.
Our most holy and great Father Eulogius was Bishop of Alexandria under the Emperor Heraclius, before Saint John the Almsgiver. On account of his virtues, and the grace which he had entered into before God, he produced many miracles, among which this seems worthy of mention. For when the most holy Archbishop Leo had written an epistle in which he commended the statutes of the Synod of Chalcedon, and Saint Eulogius had read it, he not only praised and accepted it; he even called it a pillar of orthodoxy and preached it to all. But God, wishing to do honor to both pontiffs, sent His angel, who, in the figure and appearance of the archdeacon of Saint Leo, gave thanks to Saint Eulogius because he had accepted the aforementioned epistle. Saint Eulogius, after he had conversed with him as a man, when the angel withdrew from his sight, perceived that he was an angel of God, and glorified God. D At length, serving God in many things, he departed.
187-188 On the same day: Commemoration of Saint our Father John, Bishop of Polybotum.
He is missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The fourteenth day.
Our Saint Father Auxentius was born in the East, during the reign of Theodosius the Younger, and possessed the dignity of scholarius.
Having renounced the world, he became a monk and ascended the mountain called Oxeia, near Chalcedon, and lived in quiet; and from that time the mountain was named for Saint Auxentius. He was most steadfast in his asceticism and most orthodox in his faith, struggling much against the heresy of Eutyches and Nestorius, and defending the fourth synod assembled at Chalcedon. He was esteemed by the emperors, and was loved and honored by all for his great virtue. He was also granted many miracles and the gift of prophecy; he foretold the scourges that God was about to send because of our sins, and through his prayers he averted and calmed them. Having lived in this way, he fell asleep and was buried in the monastery he had himself built, which was called Trichenaria.
On the same day. 
The contest of Saint Philemon, Bishop of Gaza. 
Missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH. The fifteenth day. 
The contest of the holy martyr Onesimus, disciple of the holy apostle Paul. C 
Saint Onesimus became a disciple of the holy apostle Paul. Being a slave to a certain Roman citizen named Philemon, he left his own master and ran to the apostle Paul, and became his disciple; and having been baptized by him, served him. For this reason the holy Paul wrote an epistle to Philemon, asking him to receive Onesimus. But after the death of the holy Paul, he was seized by Tertullus, the prefect of Rome. Brought to Puteoli, he was beaten severely with rods, and since he did not consent to deny Christ and worship idols, but rather with boldness cried out that he believed in Christ, the creator of heaven and earth and sea and all that is in them, he stirred Tertullus to anger, and having his legs crushed, particle by particle, he departed to the Lord whom he loved.
On the same day. 
The commemoration of Saint Paphnutius and the holy Euphrosyne his daughter. 
Missing.
On the same day. 
The contest of the holy martyr Major. 
He served as a soldier during the reign of the emperors Diocletian and Maximian. Having been denounced for being a Christian, he was seized in the city of Gaza and was compelled to sacrifice to the idols. But as he would not be persuaded, but rather cried out with a loud voice that he believed in his Christ and despised the lifeless idols, he provoked his captors to greater anger. And having been stretched upon the ground, his whole body was struck forcibly and torn apart, while thirty-six soldiers took turns beating him; and being lifted up half-dead, so that he scarcely had the appearance of a man because of the blood and wounds, he was thrown into prison, barely breathing. And after seven days he was brought out again, and...
sus tortured, and unwilling to abjure Christ, he was dragged by the impious command of the tyrants to the appointed place, and while praying, was beheaded.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The sixteenth day.
Contest of the holy martyrs Pamphilus, Valens, and their companions.
These, for the sake of the Christian faith, in the sixth year of the persecution of the impious Emperor Diocletian, being from various cities, stations, and ranks, were apprehended in this manner. When they were seeking Caesarea, and were about to enter the gate of the city, being asked by the guards of the gate who they were and for what reason they came, not knowing how to lie, they answered that they were Christians and that they had the heavenly Jerusalem as their fatherland. And so, immediately bound with chains, they were delivered to the prefect Firmilianus. And indeed the holy Elias, Jeremias, Isaias, Samuel, and Daniel, after many tortures, were beheaded; but Pamphilus, and Seleucius, and Valens, and Paulus were first delivered to custody, and finally they too were smitten by the sword.
On the same day.
Contest of the holy martyrs Porphyrius, Julianus, and Theodulus.
These holy ones also were companions of the holy Pamphilus, and Valens, and the rest. And Porphyrius, being a servant of the holy Pamphilus, whilst he was seeking his master's relics in order to bury them, was apprehended, and having been cast into a huge burning furnace, ended his life. The same holy Julianus, while he was kissing the relics of the saints, was seized, bound, and he too was thrown into the furnace, and there, giving thanks to God, he surrendered his soul. The holy Theodulus, however, while he was mourning for the loss of his companions and beseeching God that He might calm the most bitter persecution stirred up against His servants, was apprehended and held back; he was first tormented with most bitter tortures, then he was driven naked onto a cross, his hands and feet being pierced with long iron nails, and thus with much thanksgiving he surrendered his spirit.
On the same day.
Collection and commemoration of the relics of the holy martyrs in the city of Martyropolis, and of Maruthas the bishop.
Maruthas, a most holy bishop, having been sent by the great Theodosius, emperor of the Romans, as ambassador and apocrisiarius to the King of the Persians, was honorably received by the Persians because of the estimation they had of his virtue; especially because he had cured the king's daughter, who was oppressed by a demon, with his prayers. When they offered to him a huge sum of gold, the saint asked for nothing else but that he might take up the relics of the martyrs who had suffered in Persia, and collect them together in one place. With the Persian king consenting, the city...
built in the name of the martyrs he named Martyropolis, which is now called Mepherke; and he placed therein the relics of the holy martyrs. Much time later, Saint Maruthas himself also, by the will of God, was seized by illness and surrendered his spirit on that very same day on which he had made the gathering of the relics, with which his own precious relics were also deposited.
ON THE SAME DAY. 17
The Contest of the Holy Great Martyr Theodore the Recruit.
The holy great martyr Theodore the Recruit, in the time of the reign of Maximian and Maximin, those most bitter persecutors of the Christians, was a young man serving in that legion which they call the Recruit legion, and was enrolled under Cringas the prefect. Now, he was from the district of Amasea, and hearing that many were suffering for Christ, he was inflamed with zeal and desired himself also to endure martyrdom. Going off to Cringas in Amasea, having learned that a dragon of enormous size was near Euchaita, he reasoned with himself that if he slew the dragon, he would by all means conquer the devil as well; which he also accomplished. C Having entered Amasea, he approached Cringas, and, professing Christ, he was beaten with whips. Then, having been given time to deliberate, the saint at night set fire to the temple of the idols. Wherefore, Cringas, becoming angry, condemned Theodore to be burned by the flames with which he had burned the temple. Being cast into the furnace, he finished his struggle; and seeing his friend Cleonicus standing nearby, he said, “Cleonicus, I await you,” and thus he was brought to his end.
On the same day.
Commemoration of the Holy Mariamne, Sister of the Holy Apostle Philip.
After the ascension of Christ, the holy Philip, coming together with the holy Bartholomew and Mariamne, his own sister, to Hierapolis, and on account of his preaching the word of Christ having been hung up and having met his end, he prayed to God, and the earth was split open and swallowed up the proconsul and the people under him. Then the survivors, being terrified, began to beseech the holy Bartholomew and the holy Mariamne, who were themselves also hanging, that they might not be swallowed up along with the others; and moved by their prayers, the holy Philip not only kept them unharmed, but even brought back up those whom the earth had swallowed, leaving only the proconsul and the viper beneath the earth. Then Bartholomew and Mariamne were released; and Bartholomew, having departed to India Felix, was crucified and met his end; but the holy Mariamne, going off to Lycaonia and preaching Christ, died in peace.
On the same day.
Commemoration of our Holy Father Auxibius, Bishop of the city of Cyprus.
He was from ancient Rome, one of the pagans. Having followed the holy apostle and evangelist Mark, and having been instructed by
him in the word of truth, he was baptized and ordained deacon; then presbyter; and after this he was appointed bishop of the city of Soli in Cyprus. Having gone there and taken charge of the church, he did not surrender himself to delights and pleasures, as some do, but to vigils, prayers, sleeping on the ground, and fastings, praying to God night and day that He might have mercy on his people, and quell the persecution raised by the tyrants against the Christians, and that all might come to know the right faith and renounce impiety. Doing these things, and turning many to the living God, and having performed numberless miracles, he departed in peace to God, rejoicing forever with the rest of the saints.
On the same day. Commemoration of our holy Father Flavian, Archbishop of Constantinople.
Our holy Father Flavian was a presbyter of the holy church of Constantinople, and on account of the outstanding integrity of his life, by the will of God and the suffrage of the emperor and the senate, he was ordained patriarch of the same church. While he was pasturing the flock entrusted to him in the best manner, and instructing the people in virtues, by the permission of God he was deposed by the heretic Dioscorus and his accomplices, having held the patriarchate for one year and ten months; and he was sent into exile, where, having endured many and various hardships for the orthodox faith, and being vexed by hunger, thirst, and countless evils, since he did not cease to give thanks to God, and spent his days and nights in prayer, he finally departed to God rejoicing. And so, being tempted for the sake of the orthodox faith, he passed away.
In the same month. The eighteenth day. Commemoration of our holy Father Agapetus the Wonderworker, Bishop of Synnada.
Our holy Father Agapetus lived during the reign of the emperors Diocletian and Maximian Hercules; he was from the region of Cappadocia, born of Christian parents. Having gone into a monastery, he became a monk, and being very dear to the prefect of the monastery on account of the integrity of his life, he was instructed in the sacred letters. Having received from God the grace of miracles, he slew by his prayers a huge dragon that lived not far from the monastery, doing away with men and beasts. Driven by his fame, Licinius, seeing that he was robust and strong, conscripted him against his will and reluctance into the military. But when he had died, and Constantine the Great was reigning, he was ordained presbyter by the bishop of Synaus, and upon that man's death, he was ordained bishop of Synaus. And having performed many miracles, he ended his life in peace.
On the same day. Commemoration of our holy Father Leo, Pope of Great Rome.
This admirable Father of ours, Leo—on account of his many vir-
tues and his continence and purity, having been ordained bishop of great Rome—did indeed perform many other things worthy of his virtue; but he shone most especially in those matters which pertain to the right faith. For when the Council of the six hundred and thirty Fathers was gathered at Chalcedon against the heresy of those who spoke of one nature in Christ, since they preached the truth but the heretics would not yield to them, it pleased everyone that whatever Leo, the most holy Pope of Rome, had said, should be held as valid and steadfast. Asked therefore by the Fathers, and having prayed to God, he wrote an epistle explaining the truth, which they called the "Column of Orthodoxy," preaching two natures in Christ. After this, the great Leo, having lived most holily, rested in peace.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The nineteenth day
The contest of the holy martyrs Maximus and Theodotus.
Maximus and Theodotus, martyrs of Christ, as they traversed every city and region, preached the truth with boldness, and confessed Christ to be the true God, and Lord of all, and Creator of the world; but [they declared] idols to be the works of hands, and products of art fashioned from stone and wood. For this reason they were seized by the idolaters and delivered to the ruler of the region. By him they were most severely tortured, being compelled to deny Christ and worship idols. As they would not be persuaded, they were dragged in chains from city to city, and were thrown to wild beasts. Then they were torn with iron claws, and finally, a great furnace having been kindled, they were thrown into it rejoicing; and giving thanks, they delivered their holy and blessed souls to the Lord.
IN THE SAME MONTH
The twentieth day
The contest of the holy martyr Sadoch the bishop, and his one hundred and twenty-eight companions.
The holy Sadoch was a bishop in Persia. Because he taught the people the faith in Christ and persuaded many to come to the living and true God, he stirred up the Magi, the fire-worshippers, to anger and great bitterness. And as those [Magi] wished to send satellites to capture the saint, the same saint saw in a dream the holy martyr Simeon, his predecessor in the episcopate, sitting upon a long ladder and calling him to come to him, and saying: "The King of kings calls thee," which signified his ascent through martyrdom. And when the saint was recounting these things, the Magi who had been sent seized him together with his fellow Christians, and cast them into prison. And having reported their case to Sapor, the King of the Persians, they brought them out of prison, and having tortured them most severely, they finally beheaded them all.
On the same day
204 A Commemoration of our holy Father Sophronius the bishop.
Missing.
On the same day.
205 Commemoration of our holy Father Leo, bishop of Catania in Sicily.
Our holy Father Leo the wonderworker was from the city of Ravenna, the son of noble and wealthy parents: but being thoroughly instructed in sacred letters, and endowed with singular virtue, so that he might also perform miracles, he was ordained a priest of the church of Ravenna: then bishop of the metropolis of Catania. He fought so truly against the heretics that he convinced and confounded them all, not only by reasonings, but also by demonstrations taken from Scripture. He was a wonderworker: for he burned Heliodorus, who was deceiving many with magical arts and boasting that he could do everything, even to the point of despising fire itself, with fire through his prayers. Moved by the fame of this saint, the Roman emperors asked him, when summoned, to offer prayers to God for them; and when they had obtained that from him, they dismissed him honorably. But when he was returning to his own church, he migrated to the Lord.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
On the twenty-first day
206 Commemoration of our holy Father Agatho, bishop of Rome.
This our holy Father and wonderworker Agatho was from Italy, born of Christian and pious parents and educated by them with the greatest zeal in all the God-inspired Scriptures, from which he received so much utility and compunction of heart that, when his parents were dead, he distributed all his accumulated wealth in one day to the summoned poor, and he himself, withdrawing into a monastery and clothed in the monastic habit, dedicated himself entirely to divine worship, praying day and night for the salvation of the world. And he progressed so much in virtues that he also received the gift of miracles. But because virtue cannot be hidden, he even became Pope of Rome: and when he had conducted himself excellently in the episcopal dignity, and had left to many his own life as an example to be imitated, he fell asleep. D
IN THE SAME MONTH.
On the twenty-second day
207 Commemoration of Saint Athanasius the Confessor.
Missing.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
On the twenty-third day
208 Contest of the most holy martyr Polycarp, bishop of Smyrna.
Polycarp, the most holy disciple of John the Theologian, the apostle and evangelist, bishop of Smyrna in the place of the deceased most holy Bucolus the bishop
was ordained; for Bucolus, while still alive, had prophesied concerning him that he was to be the second bishop of Smyrna. When a persecution against the Christians broke out under the emperor Decius, he was arrested, brought before the proconsul, and after many tortures, he died. He performed many miracles; for even before he was ordained, he filled the storehouse of his nurse, which had been exhausted of all provisions, for the benefit of the needy, by pouring out prayers to God. After he was ordained, he extinguished a great conflagration through prayer and, in a time of drought, brought down rain. Having performed many other miracles by the grace of God, he fell asleep and is illustrious for the gift of miracles to this day.
OF THE SAME MONTH. THE TWENTY-FOURTH.
Commemoration of the finding of the precious head of the Forerunner.
When the holy John the Forerunner was beheaded by Herod, his head was placed in an earthenware vessel and hidden in the house of Herod. After many years, when two monks had gone to Jerusalem to worship at the tomb of the Lord, holy John appeared to them in a dream, indicating to them where his precious head lay. And when they found it, they held it in honor. From them, a certain potter received it and brought it to the city of the Emesenes. When he died, he left it to his sister; and from then on, through succession, it passed through the hands of many. In the time of the reign of Michael, who restored Orthodoxy, and of Patriarch Ignatius, it was transferred by the grace of God to Constantinople; and it was deposited in it with great honor, just as all the other holy things and the relics of the saints, for the protection and security of that city.
On the same day.
Account of holy Rheginus, bishop of Scopelos.
The holy hieromartyr Rheginus was from the region of Greece, the son of Christian parents. Having learned piety and the holy Scriptures from them, he became virtuous and God-fearing. Because of his virtue and great piety, he became a deacon in the local church in Greece, then a presbyter, and finally bishop of Scopelos. He became so renowned for his virtue that he was summoned to the synod in Sardica and, appearing there, rebuffed all the heresies by his speech and boldness. He then returned to his own diocese. After this, a persecution having been stirred up against the Christians, he was seized by the governor of Greece and, after being much tormented and punished, was beheaded by the sword, offering up his holy head.
OF THE SAME MONTH. THE TWENTY-FIFTH.
Account of the holy martyr Alexander, who suffered martyrdom in Thrace.
This man was from Puteoli, which is a city near Rome.
During the times of the emperor Maximian and the prefect Tiberianus, he was seized by the prefect and compelled to worship idols. So far was he from worshipping them that he rather loaded with insults both the idols and the prefect, saying, "They are vain, and you yourself are vain, miserable and wretched, you who believe in them." Wherefore, being suspended with his hands bound and stones attached to his feet, he was tortured; then, released, he was led to Carthage, where he was again placed upon the rack and lacerated. Being then dragged to Marcianopolis, his face was burned with torches. Finally, having bravely endured many other tortures and torments as a martyr of Christ, he went away into Thrace, where he was beheaded, receiving an incorruptible crown and eternal life from Christ our God.
OF THE SAME MONTH.
The twenty-fifth.
Commemoration of our holy Father Tarasius, Archbishop of Constantinople.
Our holy Father Tarasius was born and raised in Constantinople and held the rank of Secretary. Because of his knowledge of affairs and his great virtue, he was ordained Patriarch of Constantinople. He championed the veneration of the holy icons, and by his works, he brought it about that the Roman Empire returned to the traditions of the holy apostles and to the decrees of the seven ecumenical councils, and all the churches were reunited with the orthodox. Having lived in this manner, and being held in honor by the emperors, he built a monastery beyond the straits and established many monks there. Distinguished by his liberality toward the poor, after he had governed the Church excellently for twenty-two years and two months, he rested in peace and was buried in his own monastery.
OF THE SAME MONTH.
The twenty-sixth.
Commemoration of the holy Porphyrius, Bishop of Gaza.
The holy Porphyrius was born in the city of Thessalonica, the son of wealthy parents. Having left his parents and his wealth behind, he went into Egypt during the reign of the emperors Arcadius and Honorius and became a monk. Then, going to Jerusalem, he urged the Jews and the Greeks to join themselves to Christ, while he again carried a shoemaker's knife, sewing skins, and by that trade sustained himself and the poor. Then he was ordained presbyter by Praylus, Patriarch of Jerusalem. Finally, at the prayers of the citizens of Gaza, he was consecrated bishop of Gaza by John, Archbishop of Caesarea Philippi. Having performed many miracles, he baptized many of the Greeks and Jews; and having destroyed all the temples of the idols, he raised up churches to the saints. And having passed his life most holily in this way, he departed to God, inheriting eternal life.
On the same day
Commemoration of holy Stephen, who founded the infirmary of Armatius.
Missing.
On the same month, the twenty-seventh day
Commemoration of Procopius and Basil, the confessors, who were fellow-athletes and fellow-disciples, and who shared in the same martyrdom.
These men lived during the reign of Leo the Iconoclast. Having forsaken the world and the things in the world, they became monks; and having previously cultivated the ascetic life diligently, when the heresy against the holy images was later stirred up, they stood firmly against the Iconoclasts. Wherefore, having been arrested and vexed with many torments, since they would not yield in any way regarding the resolution of their former mind, but preached the truth until their dying breath; Basil, indeed, had his whole body and his neck flayed, and was delivered to prison. Saint Procopius was also locked away. But when the tyrant had died, they were released; and having emerged from prison, they occupied themselves with the same mode of life, and by inciting many toward virtue and recalling them to the orthodox faith, at different times they migrated to God, whom they had loved from their early youth, leaving behind their virtuous life as an excellent example to many.
On the same month, the twenty-eighth day
Commemoration of the contest of the holy martyr Nestor.
The holy martyr Nestor lived during the reign of Decius, the lawless and most unjust emperor, and under the prefect Publius. Born in the city of Perge in Pamphylia to Christian parents, and having been taught the holy Scriptures by them, he would stop the mouths of the Greeks with testimonies from the divine Scriptures, and having converted many to God, he baptized them. Having been denounced and seized by the Irenarch, the magistrate, he was brought before the prefect; and having confessed before him that Christ is the true God and Creator of the world, he was first whipped most severely; then he was flayed; and finally, as he would not be persuaded to deny Christ, he was nailed to a cross. And hanging there, he taught those who stood by the way of truth and the contempt of the present life; and thus teaching those who stood by, he breathed his last.
On the same day
Commemoration of our holy Father Thalelaeus.
He was from Cilicia. Having chosen the ascetic life, he reached the city of Gabala, and having found a certain place near the city that was a dwelling place for demons, he built a small cell there. Having put the demons to flight, he worshipped God in that same place, afflicting himself with fasts, vigils, sleeping on the ground, and the maceration of the flesh. But the demons, seeing such progress in his virtue, tried to strike him with fear; but since they were wasting their efforts, for they were forced to vanish by the prayer of Thalelaeus, driven by fury they began to uproot the trees, the olives, and the fig trees. And when the saint was in no wise moved by these things, at night with a shout
incited the saint, through the night, with voices and clamors to attack him. But when they achieved nothing, they departed defeated. He, however, having constructed a narrow cell for himself, and having entered into it, passed the whole time of his life there.
Eodem die
Commemoration of the holy women Marana and Cyra. A These holy women were from the city of Beroea in Cilicia. Having left behind both family and fatherland, and wealth, they went near to Beroea and built a small cell; and having entered within, they blocked up the door with clay and stones. And they left a small window, from which they would receive a little food and a small amount of water. And they would converse with the women who came to them, teaching them virtue. They also weighed down their whole bodies with great weights of iron; and they spoke with women only on the day of Pentecost, passing the whole remainder of the year in silence. They ate and drank only once in forty days. And since they lived in this manner piously and religiously, they were also deemed worthy of the gift of miracles; for they restored sight to the blind, healed the demon-possessed, and granted the power of walking to the lame; and thus they passed away.
Eodem die
Commemoration of Saint Domnina.
Saint Domnina was born in the city of Cyrrhus to pious and wealthy parents; burning from youth with the love of God, she devoted herself entirely to religious exercises: and in a hut which she had built from reeds contiguous to her house, she shut herself in: where, throughout the day and night, she prayed to God for the whole world, for the churches, for the orthodox emperors, for the army, for the state, and finally she made her own last prayer for her own soul. And her food was boiled lentils and a little water. Having thus lived like the angels, with her body worn down by labors, she migrated with joy to God, whom she had loved, receiving the enjoyment of eternal goods.
MENOLOGION OF THE GREEKS
PART THREE
FROM THE MONTH OF MARCH TO AUGUST.
MONTH OF MARCH.
The first day.
1 The contest of the holy martyr Eudocia the Samaritan.
Saint Eudocia, martyr of Christ, [was] from the city of Sa-
